## Triathlon Mode (MVP — behind `eventType === 'triathlon'` flag)

Triathlon is a fully-separate mode selected at onboarding. Running users are unaffected. See `docs/TRIATHLON.md` §18 for the canonical decisions backing this implementation.

### T1. Triathlon Onboarding (wizard fork)
**What it does**: New mode tile on the goals step routes triathlon users through a single consolidated `triathlon-setup` screen that collects distance (Sprint / Olympic / T100 / 70.3 / Ironman — all five plannable since 2026-06-15), race date, weekly hours, volume split across swim/bike/run (the default split is now **format-aware** — short course more swim-weighted, long course more bike-weighted), three self-rating sliders (1–5 per discipline), bike FTP + power-meter toggle, and swim CSS (or 400m test time that derives CSS). On completion, initialises `triConfig` and generates the plan. T100 offers the official PTO race calendar; Sprint/Olympic use a manual target-date path. ❌ not device-verified.

A follow-on `tri-past-race` step (inserted between `triathlon-setup` and `review`) asks whether the user completed a triathlon in the past year. If yes, they enter the distance (Sprint / Olympic / T100 / 70.3 / Ironman), month, total time, and optional per-leg splits. A "Scan Strava history" button auto-detects race days (swim+bike+run on same day in `garmin_activities`). Swim leg seeds CSS; run leg seeds VDOT (fatigue discount backed out via the `RUN_FATIGUE_DISCOUNT` table). **Race-validated run anchor (2026-06-17, SCIENCE_LOG §CC)**: the run-leg VDOT is also persisted as `s.raceRunVdot` (via the shared `raceRunLegToVdot` helper) and, while recent (≤365 days), it now **outranks a watch VO2max** for running fitness — a triathlete's watch VO2max is inflated by cycling/swimming, so a real race run leg is the stronger signal. It ranks above the device tier in `getPhysiologicalVdot` (but below a standalone-run mosaic estimate, so a better event still wins) and caps the blend's VO2 predictor (`effectiveVo2max`) across every prediction path, including the tri run-leg race time. The same anchor is refreshed when a race is later logged (`detectAndLogRaceOutcome`). The VO2 stats card still shows the true watch number; only the run predictions yield. FTP is still entered by the user (a time-only bike split can't derive watts). **Continue gate (2026-10-05)**: no distance pill starts selected on manual entry (a past race is often a different distance). A missing field is named above Continue and inside its card, which scrolls into view. Time boxes use the digit keypad. Splits read as typed: a sprint run "24:50" is mm:ss (a 2-part bike/run split reads as h:mm only while it fits inside the finish time), a swim "0:32:00" is accepted, and an unreadable split is named instead of silently dropping all three. Tests ✅ `tri-past-race-continue.test.ts` (9). ⚠️ Not device-verified.

A `tri-workout-preview` step (inserted between `tri-past-race` and `review`) showcases the full session library across four sections — swim (4 kinds), bike (9 kinds, same as standalone cycling), run (3 kinds), brick (1 kind) — laid out in a responsive 2-column grid. Cards mirror the running/cycling preview visuals (glass spec, one-liner, example structure, science citation) but are **non-interactive**: per-card opt-out would create infeasible weeks for a multi-discipline plan. Pure confidence-builder before review.

A `tri-plan-forecast` step (inserted **after** `initializing`, before `main-view` — mirroring running's `initializing → plan-preview-v2`, since the plan + prediction must exist first) is the interactive forecast reveal. **Redesigned 2026-06-16 to BE the real Forecast surface**: it reuses the shipped `renderTriForecastMarquee` (finish hero + per-leg cards) and `renderTriForecastFingerprint` (the 12-factor grid: You, Capacity, Adaptation, Training, Endurance, Durability, Bricks, Open water, Venue, Transitions, Bike & aero, Heat) verbatim, so the user lands in the app already familiar with the page. Tweakable three ways: (1) **per-discipline add-training sliders** (`.m-slider-glass`, swim / bike / run) — each +1 adds that discipline's representative session and regenerates the plan in memory, so the hero and each row's impact (`+1 ~45 min, M:SS faster`) update live; the per-discipline horizon model decides where it helps most (no invented weighting), bounded by the generator's 3-session cap; (2) **every factor chip + per-leg card is tappable**, drilling into the same explainer/editor pages the live app uses (markers, bike & aero, transitions, venue) — opened wizard-aware via `TriDetailNavOpts` (`{ onBack, showTabBar:false }`) so there's no tab bar and "← Back" returns to this step; (3) an optional **goal time** slider with a live chance-of-beating Monte Carlo. Coherence: the lever writes the regenerated plan + prediction THROUGH to the live store (`ms.wks`, `ms.triConfig.prediction`) on each committed change (volumeSplit left untouched so the slider base stays stable), so the hero, grid, and every drill-down read one `getState()` and agree. Lever state is hoisted to module scope so it survives drill-down round-trips. Continue commits authoritatively (writes onboarding split + hours, re-runs `initializeSimulator`, pins the goal to `triConfig.targetFinishTimeSec`). Lever math lives in `src/calculations/tri-session-lever.ts` (pure: `computeBumpedVolume` / `applyTriSessionDelta` / `forecastTriWithDelta` / `goalChanceTriWithDelta`). SCIENCE_LOG §AZ. ⚠️ Not device-verified.
**Key files**: `src/ui/wizard/steps/goals.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/steps/triathlon-setup.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/steps/tri-past-race.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/steps/tri-workout-preview.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/steps/tri-plan-forecast.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/race-forecast-marquee.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/race-forecast-fingerprint.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/forecast-chip-detail-view.ts` (`TriDetailNavOpts`), `src/ui/triathlon/race-forecast-leg-detail.ts`, `src/calculations/tri-session-lever.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/controller.ts`, `src/state/initialization.triathlon.ts`
**Tests**: — (UI) ⚠️ Manual test only

### T2. Triathlon Plan Engine
**What it does**: Generates a full per-phase plan (base → build → peak → taper) with swim, bike, run, brick, and optional gym sessions for each week. The week's total time budget is `triConfig.timeAvailableHoursPerWeek × phaseMultiplier`; **everything prescribed — solo swim/bike/run + brick + gym + commute — sums to at most that cap** (fixed 2026-06-16, §CB). The engine reserves gym and commute (each commute at its real `distanceKm × easyPace` duration, not a flat 60min) and the brick's `brickFrac` share, then sizes the solo sessions from `sbrMin = (capMin − gym − commute) / (1 + brickFrac)`. Per-discipline hours = `sbrHours × volumeSplit`; sessions chosen by phase + skill. Supersedes the §AM additive-commute model (commutes now count against the cap). Guard: "weekly hours cap is honoured" tests in `plan_engine.triathlon.test.ts`.

The bike library covers nine session kinds: endurance, tempo, sweet-spot, threshold, VO2 long-rep, hills, plus three pro-grade kinds — **over-unders** (alternating 105/95% FTP for lactate clearance, Coggan & Allen 2019), **VO2 micro-intervals** (Billat 30/30s for time-at-VO2max accumulation), and **VLamax neuromuscular sprints** (short max efforts for PCr/glycolytic ceiling). Phase rotation alternates classic kinds with pro-grade kinds across consecutive weeks so a multi-week build exposes the rider to both stimulus shapes.

**Ironman long-run build + key-session alternation (2026-06-17, §CD)**: the IM long run no longer tops out near 20 km. It ramps to a **2:30 (150 min) time-on-feet ceiling** (`triLongRunTargetMin`) — the durability target for a marathon run on pre-fatigued legs (Friel/Fitzgerald) — instead of taking a fixed 45% share of a bike-dominated hours budget. The **brick is now the long ride + a transition run** (the canonical IM weekend), not a separate medium brick alongside a standalone long ride: the engine pops the long ride and wraps it as the brick, and `brickFrac` drops its bike term so the freed hours fold into a longer single ride (applies to all brick distances). Across IM build/peak weeks the engine **alternates emphasis** (`triWeekEmphasis`): run-emphasis weeks run the full long run and ease the brick's ride to a 75% maintenance floor; bike-emphasis weeks do the reverse, so the Sat (brick) / Sun (long run) weekend always carries one peaked anchor and one moderate — never two maximal sessions back-to-back. The tracking durability threshold (`DURABILITY_THRESHOLDS.ironman.longRunSec`) is raised to 2.5 h to agree with the plan target. A current-week note on the plan view flags run-emphasis weeks. 70.3 and shorter keep the long-run share model. Science + constants in SCIENCE_LOG §CD.
**Race week and the race calendar (2026-10-06 to 08, generator v19 to v21, SCIENCE_LOG §CT, §CW)**: race week has no long run (the race is that week's long effort) and the scheduler keeps Sunday free in race week; the scheduler finds the long run by type (`isLongRun`), so a commute run no longer takes Sunday from the long run. Race week is the final training week whatever its phase label, so 1- and 2-week plans count too, and short plans split taper first (1 week [taper], 2 weeks [peak, taper]). Plan length is the calendar weeks from the local Monday through the week holding race day (`racePlanCalendarWeeks`, written by the setup step and recomputed in `initializeTriathlonSimulator` / `initializeCyclingSimulator`). The generator places race week on running's layout (`storedPlanRaceLayout`): the week holding the day 3 days before race day. A Monday to Wednesday race's own week is a closing week (`Week.raceClosing`, ph 'taper'): empty for a Monday race, and since 2026-10-14 (v24) holding only the day-before activation for a Tuesday or Wednesday race (next paragraphs). For a Thursday to Saturday race the week before race week keeps nothing in the 6 days before race day: the brick, long run and solo long ride (`Workout.longRide`) move to 7 or more days out in order, and the other sessions in those days leave the week (`clearStoredPlanRaceWindow`); their dose stays on the week as `Week.raceWindowCut` for the forecast only (T6). Nothing is generated on or after race day. `TRI_GENERATOR_VERSION` 21. The launch regen keeps a stored plan's length, so a plan built before 2026-10-08 keeps ending a week early when its race weekday was on or before the onboarding weekday, until it is rebuilt (ISSUE-369). Known: tri deload weeks of 7- to 9-week plans can be heavier than the week before (ISSUE-356); the tri long run does not follow running's 10% rule (ISSUE-355); race-week content for Thursday to Saturday races is cut off at race day rather than placed relative to it (ISSUE-364; partly addressed by v22, next paragraph). ❌ Not device-verified.
**Race week content (2026-10-08, generator v22, SCIENCE_LOG §CZ)**: race week keeps the week before's number of swims, rides and runs (`triRaceWeekCounts`; the race replaces the long run), each short (the 0.30 race-week budget with the 30-min floor, `TRI_SESSION_FLOOR_MIN`). It holds one quality session, the primer: the first ride, also when it is the only ride, a 30-min ride with 8 min of sweet spot or 10 min of tempo (the variant whose reps follow its minutes, `RACE_WEEK_BIKE_VARIANT`). Other rides are the steady endurance variant, never "fasted endurance (optional)"; swims are aerobic. The primer stays on Tuesday when the scheduler seats it there and Tuesday is 3 to 5 days out, otherwise it moves to the latest race-week day 3 to 5 days out (`placeStoredPlanRaceWeekPrimer`): running's primer day for a Monday to Friday race; for a Saturday or Sunday race it often lands 3 days out (Wednesday or Thursday) where running uses Tuesday (ISSUE-380). Race week never holds one discipline twice on one day: a session with no free day for its discipline is left out (a cycling race week holds at most 4 rides; race-week commutes can be left out, ISSUE-381). Sunday stays the race's slot except for a Wednesday race's primer, and the closing week of a Monday to Wednesday race holds only the race. In the last 13 days no threshold or VO2 run sits next to the brick, the long run or another quality run within the week (`spaceStoredPlanQualityRuns`); it moves to the nearest earlier day without a run, else becomes an easy run. Known: no swim primer, cycling race week 4 rides against 5 the week before, an Ironman primer the taper table does not prescribe, single-rep primer copy (ISSUE-382); spacing does not look back to the previous week (ISSUE-384); Thursday to Saturday race weeks are otherwise still the template cut at race day (ISSUE-364). `TRI_GENERATOR_VERSION` 22 for this change; 23 since 2026-10-11 (run paces from the capped run figure, next paragraph). ❌ Not device-verified. **Superseded 2026-10-14 (v24, SCIENCE_LOG §DN)**: race week is now composed from the reference week (the taper paragraph below). The frequency count, the single primer ride, the primer day and the 30-min floor above no longer describe triathlon and cycling race weeks. One session per discipline a day and the 13-day quality-run spacing still apply.

**Run paces from the capped run figure (2026-10-09/11, generator v23, SCIENCE_LOG §DA)**: the generator bakes run paces from `gp(s.v, s.lt)`, and `s.lt` now reads the triathlete run caps: the running-evidence ceiling, or the run skill VDOT in cold start (`resolveLtDanielsInput`). `initializeTriathlonSimulator` derives `s.lt` before generating the plan, and `main.ts` regenerates on a version bump after the launch refresh (`applyTriGeneratorRegen`, `src/state/tri-plan-regen.ts`), so a regen reads this launch's `s.lt`. `TRI_GENERATOR_VERSION` 23, so stored plans built from an uncapped watch LT (easy 4:24/km at watch 62, run skill 3) regenerate at the capped paces (5:46/km). Onboarding ends with the launch refresh (§DB). Tests ✅ `tri-run-lt-cap.test.ts`, `tri-plan-regen.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.
**Scheduler: no run beside the long run or brick, no weekday training-run doubles (2026-10-08, generator v22)**: no second run on the long-run day or the brick day (the brick's transition run is a run), in every week. A run that is not a commute and fits no day within its cap takes the day without a run that it overshoots least, beside a swim or ride (`placeByPreference` step 3b, `scheduler.triathlon.ts:207`), so no weekday holds two training runs while a day without a run exists. Commutes keep the older fallbacks and can share a weekday with a training run (ISSUE-378); with 0 weekday hours the weekend two-a-day can still put two runs on Saturday (ISSUE-379). Example, Ironman 10 h, default weekday hours, peak week: Monday threshold run only, Wednesday CSS swim plus easy run (was Monday threshold run plus easy run). ❌ Not device-verified.

**Race taper rebuilt from the evidence (2026-10-13/14, generator v24, lane tri-taper, `5068d8b`, SCIENCE_LOG §DN)**:
- *Length by race distance* (Tristan, 2026-10-13): sprint 1 week; Olympic, T100, 70.3 and Ironman 2; cycling 2 (`TRI_TAPER_WEEKS_BY_DISTANCE`, `triRaceTaperWeeks`). A plan too short to hold its taper plus 2 weeks keeps a one-week taper. A sprint or Olympic triathlete whose reference week holds under 8 h of training (swim, bike, run, brick run, commute runs) tapers in race week only; T100, 70.3 and Ironman keep 2 weeks at every hour setting.
- *Volume*: each taper week is a share of the reference week (the last full training week before the taper), discipline by discipline: 55% then 30%, or 55% when race week is the whole taper (`triTaperFraction`). The shares are of the reference week as the athlete trains it, effort multiplier included, and hold at multipliers 0.85 and 1.15 at the hour levels tested (Ironman at 25 to 30 h with a 1.15 multiplier sits just under, where the race-week caps bind). The CTL-gap re-anchor leaves the taper alone.
- *Taper week 1 keeps every session and its quality, with less work*: sprint and Olympic keep three quarters of the reference reps (11 × 30:30 rides, a 3 × 6 min threshold run, three quarters of the CSS or speed reps); long course holds 3 to 8 race-pace repeats of 90 s inside steady work, as many as the session's share holds; cycling keeps sweet spot or tempo rides holding three quarters of the reference ride's work. No discipline twice on one day; no fasted ride. A quality run the spacing rule would ease moves to an earlier free day and stays quality.
- *Race week*: easy sessions about 6 days out; race-pace openers 3 to 5 days out (as many 90 s repeats as days to go; a thin week still gets one, in the discipline that overshoots its budget least); for T100, 70.3 and Ironman a 60-min brick 3 days out when race week's budget holds it; nothing 2 days out; the day before a 20 to 30 min "Pre-race spin", a 5 to 10 min "Pre-race jog", or both, whichever the budget holds, always the lightest day. A Tuesday or Wednesday race's day before falls in the race's own week (the closing week), which holds that activation and the race. No session in a discipline the reference week never trains.
- *Limits*: no race-week session longer than 45 min (run, swim) or 90 min (bike), and no aerobic session longer than the reference week's average session; at most two training sessions a day, gym included; gym only 4 or more days out. Weekday and weekend hours choose the days; a session keeps its length when no day has room, as in normal weeks.
- *Frequency*: every taper week keeps a session in each discipline the reference week trains, for every race weekday. Sunday to Wednesday races keep at least 80% of the reference week's sessions in every taper week, counting the race and the day-before activations in race week. Thursday to Saturday races keep every discipline only: a Thursday race week has one training day, and the race window cuts taper week 1 (often below 80%, below the volume band and short of its quality sessions; ISSUE-364). A Friday to Sunday onboarding whose race week is the partial first week can fall short too.
- *Thin weeks*: a very thin reference week (70.3 or T100 at 4 h with gym or commutes) sits above the volume band, every taper session at the 20-min floor; low-hours one-week race weeks (sprint and Olympic at 3 to 4 h) can reach about 0.73 of the reference week for the same reason.
- *Weekly load target*: the AI Coach's planned weekly load uses the plan's shares (55% then 30%); the end-of-week settle applies them to its running part only.
`TRI_GENERATOR_VERSION` 24, so stored plans regenerate. ❌ Not device-verified.

**Key files**: `src/workouts/plan_engine.triathlon.ts` (`triTaperWeekOne`, `fitTaperSlots`, `triRaceWeek`, `spreadTaperDoubles`, `taperWeekKeepsDisciplines`, `reseatEasedQualityRuns`, `triTaperOpts`, `triTrainedReference`, `triAsTrained`, `triStateAtWeek`, `triEffortScalesWeek`), `src/workouts/tri-taper.ts` (`composeTriRaceWeek`, `composeTriRaceWeekWithClosing`, taper session builders), `src/workouts/tri-intensity-profile.ts` (`TRI_TAPER_INTENSITY_CEILING`, `CYCLING_TAPER_INTENSITY_CEILING`, `triTaperCeiling`), `src/constants/triathlon-constants.ts` (`TRI_TAPER_WEEKS_BY_DISTANCE`, `triRaceTaperWeeks`, `triTaperFraction`), `src/workouts/scheduler.triathlon.ts`, `src/workouts/swim.ts`, `src/workouts/bike.ts`, `src/workouts/brick.ts`, `src/calculations/durability-cap.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/plan-view.ts`, `src/workouts/race-week-layout.ts`, `src/workouts/race-day-rules.ts`
**Tests**: `src/workouts/plan_engine.triathlon.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (incl. IM long-run build, brick = long ride + transition run, weekly-cap conservation, race week with no long run and Sunday free for five distance and hours cases and for 1- and 2-week plans, commute long-run placement); `src/workouts/bike.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (12 tests); race calendar ✅ `src/state/race-plan-race-week.test.ts` (sprint, olympic, 70.3, Ironman, cycling and HYROX × every race weekday), `src/state/race-plan-calendar-weeks.test.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/steps/setup-plan-length.test.ts`, `src/workouts/race-window.stored-plan.test.ts`, `src/workouts/race-day-drop.tri-hyrox.test.ts`, `src/state/plan-anchor-local.test.ts`; race week content ✅ `src/state/tri-race-week.test.ts` (scenario tri-wednesday, no doubles at 0 weekday hours, quality-run spacing, every distance incl. T100 × cycling × race weekday × hours); scheduler ✅ `src/workouts/scheduler.triathlon.test.ts` (no run beside the long run or brick, step 3b unit, real-plan sweep of 4,050 plans asserting no weekday with two non-commute runs, Ironman 10 h at the default split); race taper ✅ `src/state/tri-taper-rules.test.ts` (43: distances × 2 to 24 weeks × hours × weekday hours × race weekday, gym and commutes down to the slider's lowest hours, known Thursday to Saturday gaps pinned, taper length, forecast fallback, CTL-gap stamp and effort multipliers 0.85 and 1.15), `src/state/tri-taper-effort.test.ts` (7: multipliers set by ratings across 5 modes, Sunday and Wednesday races; rollover walk into a Tuesday or Wednesday closing week), `src/workouts/tri-taper.test.ts` (32: composer, quality builders, activations, intensity gate). ❌ Not device-verified.

### T2b. Cycling-only mode (V1) — ⚠️ HIDDEN from picker (2026-05-29)
**Status**: The cycling tile is hidden from the onboarding mode picker via `HIDDEN_MODES` in `goals.ts` (parked: single-discipline V1, not a shipping priority, and no run leg so the commute model doesn't apply). All cycling code paths, the tile definition, and the routing branches are intact — re-enable by removing `'cycling'` from `HIDDEN_MODES`. Existing cycling-mode state continues to work. The rest of this section describes the (still-functional) path.

**What it does**: Standalone cycling plan path alongside running and triathlon. Goals screen has a four-tile mode picker; cycling users go through `connect-data → cycling-setup → workout-preview → review → initializing`. Cycling setup collects event distance (50/100/160/200/300 km Gran Fondo class), target date, weekly hours, and strength sessions. Workout-preview renders the 9 bike kinds with literature anchors as a research showcase before plan generation. Plan engine reuses `generateTriathlonPlan` with `triConfig.disciplines = ['bike']`; swim/run/brick generation is gated. Bike session cap lifts from 3 to 5 in single-discipline mode.

**Cycling finish-time prediction**: `predictCyclingEvent` (`src/calculations/race-prediction.cycling.ts`) computes finish time using FTP × distance-keyed intensity factor (IF from Allen & Coggan 2010: 50 km→0.88, 100 km→0.80, 160 km→0.74, 200 km→0.68, 300 km→0.62) and the Martin et al. 1998 bike physics engine. Uses full physics when aero profile + masses are set; falls back to a linear watts→kph approximation otherwise. Confidence range ±10% >4 weeks out, ±6% within 4 weeks. Card shown in Forecast tab via `renderCyclingFinishCard`.

**Race calendar (2026-10-08)**: cycling plans take the triathlon engine's race calendar (T2): plan length through the week holding the event (`racePlanCalendarWeeks` in `cycling-setup.ts` and `initializeCyclingSimulator`), race week on running's layout, a closing week for a Monday to Wednesday event (empty for a Monday event; since 2026-10-14 the pre-race spin the day before a Tuesday or Wednesday event), the long ride moved out of the 6 days before a Thursday to Saturday event, nothing on or after event day. `computeWeeksToRace` measures to `raceForecastHorizonISO`, so a Monday to Wednesday event's range matches the Sunday before. Tests ✅ `race-plan-race-week.test.ts`, `race-closing-week-forecast.test.ts`, `setup-plan-length.test.ts`.

**Race taper (2026-10-13/14, lane tri-taper, `5068d8b`, SCIENCE_LOG §DN)**: cycling tapers 2 weeks at every hour setting. Taper week 1 keeps all rides at 55% of the reference week and keeps its quality rides as sweet spot or tempo holding three quarters of the reference ride's work (`CYCLING_TAPER_INTENSITY_CEILING`). Race week is 30% of the reference week: a race-pace opener ride on each day 5 to 3 days out when the budget holds it (90 s repeats at the event's intensity factor, `CYCLING_EVENT_INTENSITY_FACTOR`), aerobic rides, nothing 2 days out, and a 20 to 30 min pre-race spin the day before (no jog). Tests ✅ `tri-taper-rules.test.ts`, `tri-taper-effort.test.ts`, `tri-taper.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

**Cycling plan view**: discipline mini-bars show only the bike bar (full-width) in cycling mode, labelled "Hours this week". Week navigation pills let users preview future weeks without changing the live week. Future weeks show a draft banner and block workout card taps.

**Key files**: `src/ui/wizard/steps/cycling-setup.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/steps/workout-preview.ts`, `src/state/initialization.cycling.ts`, `src/workouts/plan_engine.triathlon.ts` (discipline gate), `src/ui/wizard/controller.ts` (cycling routing branch), `src/calculations/race-prediction.cycling.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/race-forecast-card.ts` (`renderCyclingFinishCard`)
**Tests**: ⚠️ Manual test only (V1 — first dogfood pass to confirm wizard flow). See `docs/TESTER.md` Profiles A–C.

### T3. Multi-sport Transfer Matrix
**What it does**: Every activity contributes to its own discipline's CTL and ATL at 1.0, and to other disciplines at reduced weights (§18.3). Generalises the old `runSpec` discount to support any sport. Drives per-discipline CTL when a padel / ski / strength session lands on the activity log.
**Key files**: `src/constants/transfer-matrix.ts`, `src/calculations/fitness-model.triathlon.ts`
**Tests**: `src/calculations/fitness-model.triathlon.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (10 tests)

### T4. Per-discipline CTL / ATL / TSB
**What it does**: Independent 42-day CTL + 7-day ATL tracks for swim, bike, run. Combined CTL is a weighted sum (COMBINED_CTL_WEIGHTS). Per-discipline ACWR for discipline-specific overload detection. Same Banister 1975 exponential-decay EMAs as running mode.

**New athletes: days before plan start are unknown, not rest (2026-10-07, decision 1)**: when no activity of any sport predates plan start, readiness counts only the days since then (`triLoadObservedDays`, stored as `triConfig.fitness.loadObservedDays`). `observedDisciplineLoad` rescales each discipline's decay-weighted sum to the observed days, and `perDisciplineAcwrRaw` (the one ratio behind tri readiness, the displayed workload ratio and the discipline floor) stays unknown until 14 observed days. A new triathlete or cyclist training steadily no longer reads Overreaching for the first month, and the under-3-weeks history gate applies as for a new runner. An athlete with any earlier record keeps the existing calculation, so a new sport's ramp still warns. Stored CTL/ATL/Form display values are unchanged. Tests ✅ `tri-readiness-observed.test.ts`.

**Sessions without heart rate (2026-10-07, decision 8a)**: tri fitness values an HR-less session at minutes × `TL_PER_MIN[rated RPE]` (RPE 5 unrated), the same as the weekly and daily load numbers (`triActivityTss`, `tri-benchmarks-from-history.ts:1057`; ratings attached by `withTriRatedRpe`). It used a private scale (unrated hour: swim 54, ride 60, run 66, other 36 TSS, against 69). HR-less "other sport" load rises about 1.9×, so the cross-training readiness bar and the cross-dominant readiness sentence can move. Tests ✅ `tri-load-no-hr.test.ts`, `load-currency-anchors.test.ts` Anchor 10.

**Key files**: `src/calculations/fitness-model.triathlon.ts`, `src/calculations/tri-benchmarks-from-history.ts`
**Tests**: covered in T3 tests ✅

### T5. Swim / Bike TSS
**What it does**: Swim TSS uses cubed IF (water drag ∝ v³; Toussaint & Beek 1992). Bike TSS uses squared IF when power meter present; HR-reserve fallback otherwise.
**Key files**: `src/calculations/triathlon-tss.ts`
**Tests**: `src/calculations/triathlon-tss.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (13 tests)

### T6. Triathlon Race Prediction
**What it does**: Predicts total race time and per-leg splits for all five formats (Sprint / Olympic / T100 / 70.3 / Ironman). Swim uses CSS + 5s/100m race-pace offset (Dekerle 2002). Bike derives speed from FTP × race IF (`RACE_INTENSITY_BY_DISTANCE`, per-format) or skill fallback. Run uses VDOT (Daniels) plus a per-format pace fatigue discount (`RUN_FATIGUE_DISCOUNT` table: sprint 2% / olympic 4% / t100 5% / 70.3 5% / IM 11%; Bentley 2007, Landers 2008, with the three short/middle values extrapolated — SCIENCE_LOG §BU). T1/T2 estimated from skill slider. Confidence band ±8–10%. Sprint and Olympic are now plannable targets in their own right; the predictor still also shows side-effect estimates at other distances. **Per-leg pace caption (added 2026-06-01)**: under each leg time the card shows the implied split — swim pace per 100m, bike speed (km/h or mph) plus the model's target watts (`p.bikeAvgWatts = ftp × RACE_INTENSITY_BY_DISTANCE`), run pace per km/mi. Speed/pace are derived from the *displayed* leg seconds so the caption always agrees with the time above it; watts come from the prediction so the figure isn't recomputed off a duplicated intensity constant. Unit-aware via `state.unitPref`. Single helper `legSplitLine()` lives in `race-forecast-card.ts` and is **shared by both per-leg surfaces**: the Stats marquee/cycling card (`race-forecast-card.ts` `legCell`) and the Forecast-tab marquee (`race-forecast-marquee.ts` `legCard`). When changing the split format, both call sites update from the one helper.

**Leg-breakdown drill-down (added 2026-06-01)**: tapping a Swim / Bike / Run leg on the Forecast-tab marquee opens a waterfall page (`src/ui/triathlon/race-forecast-leg-detail.ts`, `renderTriLegBreakdown(leg)`) that decomposes the projected leg time into ordered stages: base marker (CSS+5s / target watts / VDOT+brick-fatigue) → course factors → heat credit → durability cap → race-day readiness → past-race calibration. Each row shows the cumulative time and the delta the step applied; the last row reconciles to the leg time on the card because both read `prediction.legWaterfall` (built in `race-prediction.triathlon.ts` by `buildLegWaterfall` from intermediate stages emitted by `computeRaceTime`). Steps under 3s are hidden. The course-factors row carries provenance inline (`{Venue} course factors` + `Calibrated, {n} finishers` for empirical venues, or `Physical course model` otherwise) via `prediction.courseMeta`, so a big venue penalty reads as a calibrated number rather than a mystery. Factor rows (open water, course, heat, durability, calibration) deep-link to the matching forecast factor chip; the base row links to Capacity. Below the card, a plain-language course note for that leg (reuses the exported `legCommentary(leg, deltaSec, profile)` from `race-forecast-card.ts`) explains the character in words — e.g. swim "Open lake swim with exposed wind. Chop and sighting slow this leg." It is derived from the venue's physical attributes (swim type, profile, wind, climate, elevation) and the direction of the course delta, and returns '' rather than speculate when the attributes can't credibly explain the gap (no invented reputation claims). Same helper drives swim/bike/run. This is the leg-first cut of the same data the factor chips show factor-first, not a parallel computation. Page chrome mirrors the chip drill-down (`forecast-chip-detail-view.ts`). Reconciliation is unit-tested (`race-prediction.triathlon.test.ts`: last step == leg sec within rounding).

**Race-readiness penalty (added 2026-05-06, bike/swim recency extended 2026-05-07)**: per-discipline endurance-volume penalty applied to the *current* ("if you raced today") prediction. Recognises that fitness markers (FTP/CSS/VDOT) describe physiological capacity but don't account for the specific endurance preparation a long-course race requires. An athlete with strong markers but low recent swim/bike/run volume gets the today-prediction lowered (max 15% for IM, 10% for 70.3, 5% for Olympic, 3% for Sprint) — but the race-day projection is unchanged because by race day the plan has delivered the volume. Run discipline carries forward the existing PB-recency reduction (recent race PB → halved penalty; continuous in age since 2026-10-12: 0.5 through the first year, linear to 0.75 at two years and the full penalty at three, `pbRecencyPenaltyFactor`, SCIENCE_LOG §DE, also for the bike and swim credit). **Bike/swim extended 2026-05-07**: a recently completed triathlon reduces the bike and swim penalties via cross-distance credit weights (`TRI_DISTANCE_CROSS_CREDIT` in `race-readiness-targets.ts`) — an IM finish fully credits all distances; shorter races give partial credit upward. See SCIENCE_LOG "Triathlon Bike/Swim PB-Recency Cross-Credit 2026-05-07".

**Forecast projects at the planned dose, not the next 4 weeks (2026-06-17, SCIENCE_LOG §CG)**: the race-day *projection* understands the training you're going to do, read straight from the planned weeks (the full base→build→peak→taper ramp lives in `state.wks[w].triWorkouts`). Two terms, each fed the volume it's a function of: the fitness-gain horizon reads the **build-phase mean** dose (`plannedBuildDoseByDiscipline` — average across remaining non-taper weeks), and the race-readiness penalty closure reads the **peak the plan holds** (`plannedPeakDoseByDiscipline` — mean of peak-phase weeks). Previously both sampled a fixed next-4-week window, which early in a plan sits in the low-volume base phase and under-credited the build — the projected bike/run got dragged by the durability cap (a demonstrated ~36 km/h cyclist forecast at ~34) because the durability execution factor stayed low. No invented constants. Taper weeks are excluded from both doses (freshness, not build), and the taper invariant (projected per-leg == current inside the taper window) is preserved. The Forecast training chip (`forecast-chip-detail-view.ts`) shows the "Average build week" dose and a caption noting the readiness penalty closes at the peak-week rate. ✅ unit-tested (`tri-volume-by-discipline.test.ts`, +10), ❌ not device-verified.

**Forecast follows the race calendar (2026-10-08, SCIENCE_LOG §CW)**: the planned dose reads the plan's build prescription on every path (build mean, peak mean, and the 4-week fallback once only taper weeks remain, `forecastLookAheadDose`). Sessions the race window removes from the week before race week (`Week.raceWindowCut`) count as that week's dose, so a Thursday to Saturday race forecasts like its plan without the window on every plan week. A Monday to Wednesday race's horizon ends with race week (`raceForecastHorizonISO`, the Sunday before race day), so it forecasts exactly like the Sunday before while training weeks remain (within 0.5% once only taper weeks remain, since 2026-10-14); before `1ff0194` an Ironman on Wednesday 4 Nov 2026 read 8.5 min faster than Sunday 1 Nov. The Training chip fingerprint (`plannedSessionsPerWeekByDiscipline`, next 4 weeks) shows the plan as scheduled, without the cut and skipping the closing week; the chip detail page ("Engine inputs from your plan") reads `plannedBuildDoseByDiscipline`, so its planned sessions per week include the cut sessions. Cycling's range narrows on weeks to the end of race week. In a closing week the training horizon is 0, so the Training detail header reads "Race date pending" (ISSUE-366). The peak mean still includes 4-week-cycle deload weeks (ISSUE-368). Tests ✅ `src/state/race-window-forecast.test.ts`, `src/state/race-closing-week-forecast.test.ts`, `src/calculations/tri-volume-by-discipline.test.ts` (cut and closing-week cases). ❌ Not device-verified.

**Forecast reads the plan's taper (2026-10-13/14, lane tri-taper, `5068d8b`, SCIENCE_LOG §DN)**: the taper weeks the forecast subtracts from useful build time, and the horizon's `taper_weeks`, are the plan's own race taper for swim, bike and run alike (`triPlanTaperWeeks`, `race-week-layout.ts:248`). Before the plan's weeks exist (onboarding) it applies the plan engine's rules: low hours for sprint and Olympic (`triTaperOpts`), the short-plan rule, and the training weeks of the generator's layout, so a closing week is left out. The per-discipline `TRI_TAPER_WEEKS` table it replaced (swim 1 to 2.5, bike 0.5 to 2, run 1 to 3 weeks) disagreed with the plan. Effect on long-course low-hours plans that already tapered 2 weeks, science reviewer's probe at `ba5e09b` (16 weeks, Sunday race): Ironman 7 h 27 s faster, 70.3 7 h 8 s slower, T100 6 h 5 s slower. A Monday to Wednesday race forecasts exactly like the Sunday before while training weeks remain and within 0.5% once only taper weeks remain, because the two race weeks hold the same budget on different days. Tests ✅ `tri-taper-rules.test.ts` (forecast taper equals the plan's, with and without the weeks built), `race-closing-week-forecast.test.ts`, `race-window-forecast.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

**Course-factors panel layout (updated 2026-05-11)**: empirical (calibrated) and physical (Tier-A fallback) course factors render with the same shape — one row per leg. Empirical mode shows a `legCommentary` description derived from the venue's published physical attributes (`swimType`, `bikeProfile`/`runProfile`, elevation, altitude, wind exposure, climate); commentary is honest where physics alone doesn't explain the empirical signal and stays quiet rather than speculate (no claims about field strength). Physical mode aggregates per-dimension entries (Climate, Run elevation, Bike elevation, Swim type, Wind) into a single per-leg delta with the contributing dimensions listed underneath ("Climate · Run elevation"); single-dimension legs show the raw value (e.g. "Climate · Hot-humid (~30°C)"). Negative deltas (faster) render in `var(--c-ok)` green, matching the per-leg breakdown card above. Below the rows: a quiet `N finishes from this venue.` footer (empirical mode only), plus an altitude-acclimatisation warning for venues ≥800 m (we don't track where the athlete trains, so we surface the venue altitude and let them weight it). The calibration caption at the bottom of the parent card distinguishes empirical vs physical-model venues honestly — empirical races cite the 1.3M-finish CoachCox/Kaggle calibration; physical-model races state the dataset doesn't cover that venue. Mode detection lives in a single `isEmpiricalFactors()` helper so the value-format contract is in one place.

**Swim-environment normalisation (added 2026-05-09)**: every swim activity carries a `swimEnvironment` tag (`pool` / `wetsuit-lake` / `non-wetsuit-lake` / `ocean` / `river`). CSS estimation normalises each swim's pace to a wetsuit-lake baseline by dividing by `SWIM_TYPE_MULTIPLIER[env]` before pooling, so pool-trained CSS and OW-trained CSS are pooled honestly into one number. Race-prediction then re-applies the course factor for the actual race environment without double-counting. Pool swims auto-tag from `activityType`; OW swims fall back to `triConfig.swim.defaultOwSwimEnvironment` (set once via the reveal modal). Pool coefficient = 1.00 — pool→OW penalty (~+5–10%) approximately cancels wetsuit benefit (~−5–6%), so a pool swim ≈ a wetsuit-lake swim at race effort. Cited in `triathlon-course-factors.ts:154` block (Toussaint 1989, Cordain 1991, de la Fuente Pacheco 2020, Baldassarre 2017, López-Belmonte 2024). One-time reveal modal (`src/ui/triathlon/swim-normalisation-reveal.ts`) explains the work to users on first visit to the forecast view and captures their OW default in the same dismissal. Tests: 3 new in `tri-benchmarks-from-history.test.ts` (61 total ✅).

**Race-readiness UI surface (added 2026-05-06)**: summary card on the triathlon Forecast page (between retro card and race-day-target card). Three discipline bars (swim/bike/run) with 0-100 readiness scores. Tap-through to detail view with per-discipline breakdown — volume actual vs target, longest session actual vs target, plain-tone coaching line ("Build weekly volume by ~3 hrs/wk to match the race target"), PB-recency note (run: from race PBs; bike/swim: from completed triathlons since 2026-05-07). Cards/bars/colours mirror the daily-readiness pattern in `src/ui/readiness-view.ts:479-512` for visual consistency.

**Rounded once, capped run figure, PB age (2026-10-09 to 12, SCIENCE_LOG §DA, §DC, §DD)**: `computeRaceTime` returns unrounded legs and `predictTriathlonRace` rounds once at its output, so a smooth drift ticks whole-second times by at most 2 s a day (was 3). The run leg reads the capped run figure in cold start and the age-weighted PB blend under a measured volume. The Ironman marathon-depth credit ages the marathon PB with the PB age weight (1 year 0.79, 2 years 0.63, 4 years 0.40, replacing the §T5 bands), outside the measured-volume gate (ISSUE-388). The triathlon Forecast carries the PB-age caption when ageing moves the run leg by at least 1 s. A 70.3 run leg gets faster as a half PB ages (ISSUE-387). Tests ✅ `pb-age-continuity.test.ts` (Ironman smoothness), `tri-run-lt-cap.test.ts`, `pb-age-caption.test.ts`.

**Key files**: `src/calculations/race-prediction.triathlon.ts`, `src/calculations/specific-endurance-penalty.ts`, `src/calculations/race-readiness.ts`, `src/constants/race-readiness-targets.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/race-forecast-card.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/race-readiness-card.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/race-readiness-detail.ts`
**Tests**: `src/calculations/race-readiness.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (20 tests: per-discipline scoring, penalty bands, geometric mean, PB recency, aggregator); core race-prediction logic ⚠️ not yet covered

**Prediction calibration loop (added 2026-05-11)**: per-user systematic bias correction derived from `triConfig.raceLog`. Three-tier ladder: tier 0 (< 2 races) = no change; tier 1 (2+ races) = per-leg additive bias (median actual − predicted, ±8% cap); tier 2 (4+ races with `predictedRawPerLeg`) = Bayesian-shrunk readiness maxPenalty scale per leg; tier 3 (6+ races spanning 3+ distances) = dormant until sprint/olympic logging is added. Calibration updates after each logged race and backfills on first launch when raceLog exists without calibration. Forecast view shows a faint caption when tier ≥ 1. First tier-up surfaced once in the week-debrief plan-preview step. **Key file**: `src/calculations/tri-calibration.ts` ✅ 14 tests.

**Race-outcome logging across modes (added 2026-05-12)**: parity pass to mirror tri's race outcome logger into running and HYROX (CLAUDE.md mirror rule). Running stores entries on `state.runRaceLog[]` with predicted from `state.blendedRaceTimeSec`; HYROX stores on `hyroxConfig.raceLog[]` using `targetFinishTimeSec` as the "predicted" slot since HYROX has no continuously cached prediction. Both detectors run idempotently after Strava sync via a new `detectRaceOutcomeAfterSync()` dispatcher in `main.ts`. **Key files**: `src/calculations/run-race-outcome.ts`, `src/calculations/hyrox-race-outcome.ts`.

**Race-outcome retro UI (added 2026-05-18)**: small green-tinted "you beat your prediction/target by X" card on the Race Forecast page in running mode (`buildRunRaceOutcomeRetroCard` in `src/ui/race-forecast-view.ts`, above the hero ring) and on the Forecast tab in HYROX mode (`renderHyroxRaceOutcomeRetroCard` in `src/ui/hyrox/forecast-view.ts`, between page title and body). Mirrors tri's `renderRaceOutcomeRetroCard` pattern (`triathlon/forecast-view.ts:42`). Renders only when actual was ≥60s faster than predicted (running) or target (HYROX) — celebratory-only, same gate as tri. **Cycling-only retro** deferred as ISSUE-224 (needs its own `cyclingRaceLog` field). **HYROX station-level retro** deferred as ISSUE-223 (needs `HyroxRaceLogEntry` extension to capture per-station actuals). Running calibration loop (analogue of `tri-calibration.ts`) also still deferred.

### T7. Discipline-aware Activity Matcher + Brick Detection
**What it does**: Matches synced swim / bike / run activities to planned `triWorkouts` — discipline first, then same-day, then nearest duration. Brick detector flags bike → run pairs where the run starts within 30 min of bike end (§18.1). After matching, each workout stores `matchedActivityId` so the effort multiplier can look up objective signals on the matched actual.

**Bike effort scoring (post-match)**: `scoreTriEffort` computes power adherence (NP / (FTP × target IF)) with a per-session-type tolerance band (±5–10%) applied as a continuous soft gradient — maximum 50% attenuation at centre, zero at band edge, no discontinuity. Power adherence stored as `GarminActual.powerAdherence`. HR effort score computed as fallback for rides without a power meter.

**Watt prescription bands**: All bike workout descriptions show a target range (e.g. "135–155W") instead of a point. Band constants in `BIKE_ADHERENCE_BAND` (triathlon-constants.ts): ±10% endurance/vlamax, ±8% tempo/hills/VO2/micros, ±6% sweet spot, ±5% threshold/over-unders. Applies equally to standalone cycling and triathlon bike sessions.
**Key files**: `src/calculations/activity-matcher.triathlon.ts`, `src/calculations/brick-detector.ts`, `src/calculations/tri-effort-scoring.ts`
**Tests**: `src/calculations/tri-effort-scoring.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (14 tests: on-target, band attenuation, edge continuity, outside-band passthrough, null paths, HR fallback)

### T7b. Per-discipline Effort Multiplier (bike / swim / run adaptive scaling)
**What it does**: After each completed week, `triTrailingEffortScore` computes a trailing 2-week effort deviation per discipline. This scales the duration of upcoming sessions of that discipline (±15% cap), mirroring running's `effortMultiplier` in `plan_engine.ts`.

**Bike signal blend**: power adherence (60%) + RPE (40%) when a power meter is present; HR effort score (60%) + RPE (40%) as fallback; pure RPE when neither is available. All signals are converted to the same deviation scale as RPE (0.1 adherence offset ≈ 1 RPE point). This matches running's HR+RPE blend but promotes power as the primary signal since cycling HR lags interval power by 30–60s.

**Swim / run**: pure RPE for swim (no reliable real-time pace signal yet); run reuses the running-side HR+RPE blend from `events.ts`.

**Completed weeks only (2026-10-06, `fed8bfc`)**: `triTrailingEffortScore` starts its lookback at the last completed week (`wks[s.w - 2]`), as running's `getTrailingEffortScore` does; it started at the week in progress, so a mid-week regeneration moved on partial ratings. Known gap: `applyTriEffortMultipliers` scales minutes without re-pricing the stored load (ISSUE-338).

**Key files**: `src/calculations/effort-multiplier.triathlon.ts` (`triTrailingEffortScore`, `triEffortMultiplier`, `applyTriEffortMultipliers`)
**Tests**: `src/calculations/brick-detector.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (5 tests). `effort-multiplier-completed-weeks.test.ts` ✅. Matcher ⚠️ not yet covered.

### T7c. Triathlon / cycling plan-change suggestions (readiness, RPE, cross-training overload)
**What it does**: `collectTriSuggestions` (`tri-suggestion-aggregator.ts`) gathers proposals for the current week (`wks[s.w - 1]`; five detectors read next week before 2026-10-06) and `showTriSuggestionModal` presents them; `applyTriSuggestions` (`tri-suggestion-apply.ts`) applies the accepted ones. Triathlon (all distances) and cycling run it; HYROX does not.
- **Downgrade**: one rung down the discipline's canonical ladder. Runs step `RUN_PACE_LADDER` (threshold to marathon pace), bikes `BIKE_INTENSITY_LADDER` (threshold, sweet spot, tempo), swims `SWIM_INTENSITY_LADDER`, with RPE, description targets (pace, watts, CSS pace) and planned load re-derived (`loadForRung`, `loadForTriRun`, `src/workouts/rung-load.ts`). Since 2026-10-07 off-ladder kinds land on `OFF_LADDER_DOWNGRADE_RUNG`: over-under and hills to sweet spot, VO2 micros to threshold, open water to an endurance swim, with the description rebuilt by the generator's builders. A step that would add planned load is not offered, which holds back neuromuscular sprints (vlamax): the approved vlamax to sweet spot mapping is not shipped (ISSUE-335).
- **Swap to easy**: the easy rung (endurance ride, endurance swim, easy run, or long for the long-run slot) at 75% of the minutes, never raising intensity. **Trim**: 85% of the minutes, load scaled in proportion. All three set status 'reduced' (Adjusted chip), keep the session on Home Today, re-price it and move the Adjust overlay's baseline with it.
- **What is proposed**: only changes the apply step makes (`canApplyTriSuggestion`): a session needs planned minutes, and a swap or downgrade needs a ladder (or an off-ladder landing rung for a downgrade). Bricks and swaps on off-ladder kinds are not offered. One row per session (`oneRowPerSession`: swap beats downgrade beats trim); one Apply changes a session at most once. The readiness row and the overload builders share one quality classifier (`isTriQualitySession`). The overload modal's "-N TSS" for a downgrade is the real change (`triDowngradeTssReduction`).
- **Overload budget (2026-10-21, §DP)**: the planned TSS a cross-training proposal may cut is `overloadReductionBudget` (running's slope-1 `saturateCredit`, TAU 491), never more than the unanswered load (load-currency anchor 4b). It used a curve with slope 1.875 that let 50 TSS of tennis unlock about 91 TSS of cuts.
- **What counts as cross-training**: anything this week whose id is not a `wk.triWorkouts` session and not a planned session stored in `adhocWorkouts` (`isPlannedAdhocId`: user-added, injury capability offers, holiday, benchmark), with a twin record counted once. Each counts its iTRIMP or stamped load; one with neither (a GPS extra run, an HR-less session) counts Signal B's figure for it (`signalBActualTSS` / `signalBAdhocTSS`, 2026-10-21; it counted 0 before).
- **Notify-once**: answered rows and answered cross-training load are recorded on `Week.triSuggestionsHandled` and not proposed again that week; a new session later in the week is judged on its own against the 15% overload threshold. Sessions already changed, done or dropped are never targeted (`isOpenForTriSuggestion`).
- **Run paces** retarget from the generator's easy pace (`triRunEasyPaceSec`, `src/workouts/tri-run-pace.ts`).
- **Volume-ramp suggestions are off** (`TRI_VOLUME_RAMP_SUGGESTIONS_LIVE = false`) until a comparison window is chosen (ISSUE-337).

**Key files**: `src/calculations/tri-suggestion-aggregator.ts`, `src/calculations/tri-suggestion-apply.ts`, `src/calculations/tri-suggestion-handled.ts`, `src/calculations/tri-cross-training-overload.ts`, `src/calculations/tri-rpe-flag.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/tri-suggestion-modal.ts`, `src/constants/triathlon-constants.ts` (`OFF_LADDER_DOWNGRADE_RUNG`)
**Tests**: ✅ `tri-suggestion-apply.test.ts`, `tri-suggestion-applicable.test.ts`, `tri-suggestion-aggregator.test.ts`, `tri-cross-training-overload.test.ts`, `tri-rpe-flag.test.ts`, `tri-suggestion-modal.test.ts`, `tri-today-swapped-session.test.ts`, `workout-adjust-after-suggestion.test.ts`, `rung-load.test.ts`, `tri-run-pace.test.ts`. ⚠️ Not device-verified. ### T8. Triathlon UI (Plan / Home / Stats)
**What it does**: Three views with a dedicated minimal tab bar. Plan shows day-by-day cards with discipline-coloured stripes (swim teal, bike clay, run sage), and supports drag-and-drop reorder within the week — drop a card on another card to swap days, drop on the day-header strip to stack onto that day, or drop on an empty rest row to move there. On iOS the drag arms only after a 350ms long-press (`TOUCH_DRAG_HOLD_MS`) so scrolling doesn't reorder days by accident; a tiny "Hold and drag to reorder" hint sits above Monday on the current week. Bricks are atomic single workouts so they always move as one. The weekly summary strip at the top of the Plan view shows Weekly hours / Sessions / Week load with a small muted "X done" caption beneath each headline on the current and past weeks; each KPI now carries a 3px neutral progress bar (`renderKpiBar` in `plan-view.ts`) showing completed ÷ planned at a glance. The swim/bike/run pills underneath act as progress bars (fill = completed minutes ÷ planned minutes, right-side label `Xh / Yh`). **Missed sessions** on the current week (past day, not completed, no matched activity) show an amber "Missed" pill and, when tapped, open a detail modal with two CTAs: "Move to next week" (filled-black) and "Skip session" (transparent bordered). Move clones the workout to next week and marks the original `'skipped'`; Skip drops it with adherence penalty. A clone's `pushCount` carries forward so a lineage can be pushed at most once — after that only Skip is available. Future weeks render planned-only. Each day card now also carries a per-session status chip top-right (Logged/Done green, Missed orange, Today terracotta, Adjusted/Skipped/Replaced/Holiday/Added) via the shared `src/ui/workout-status.ts` helper, so a completed, missed, and upcoming session are distinguishable at a glance — matching the running plan card. Home shows today's workouts as separate glass cards (shared `buildHeroSessionCard` — `backdrop-filter` + faint ring + light title, same material as the running hero) with clear air between them; `buildTriathlonSessionBlock()` renders each swim/bike/run/strength session with its discipline badge and an `RPE · TSS` footer, the "DAY · Today" eyebrow on the top card only. Each card keeps the `tri-hero-card` class + `data-tri-workout-id` so taps open the workout detail modal. Home also shows per-discipline fitness bars with CTL/ATL readout, race forecast, and upcoming sessions. Stats mirrors the running stats summary → drill-down pattern: marquee race forecast card at the top (full per-leg breakdown + course factors + bike & aero entry), then a compact **Fitness summary card** (three rows showing Swim CSS · Bike FTP · Run LT pace with their tier label, tap → Fitness detail), then a **More stats →** link that opens the Progress detail page. Fitness detail (`src/ui/triathlon/fitness-detail-view.ts`) holds the "Your Numbers" zone-position bars for CSS / FTP / LT pace with per-discipline adaptation captions above each bar, three benchmark trend sparklines, and a Current Race Estimates card (Sprint / Olympic / target distance). Progress detail (`src/ui/triathlon/progress-detail-view.ts`) holds the per-discipline stat list (distance, sessions, longest, plan adherence, time, total TSS), Phase Timeline, per-discipline CTL chart, and a unified Weekly load (TSS) chart with Total + Swim/Bike/Run lines. Both charts walk archived `previousPlanWks` so the x-axis aligns. Per-discipline weekly km charts were removed as redundant (TSS already covers load); the Recent weeks drill-down keeps a per-week list of done vs planned hours / sessions / TSS. Since 2026-10-08 the Plan header's "Week N of M" names the week being viewed (it read the current week while the phase label read the viewed week; test ✅ `ui/plan-header-viewed-week.test.ts`), race day shows the display-only race-day card, and Home's Today card shows the race on race day when no session sits on that day (feature 8.3).
**Key files**: `src/ui/triathlon/plan-view.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/home-view.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/stats-view.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/fitness-detail-view.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/progress-detail-view.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/benchmark-charts.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/tab-bar.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/workout-card.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/race-forecast-card.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/colours.ts`, `src/calculations/plan-adherence.triathlon.ts`
**Tests**: — (UI) ⚠️ Manual test only

### T8a. Triathlon forecast fingerprint (chip-based factor view)
**What it does**: Six factor chips beneath the predicted finish time on the triathlon forecast page — parity with running's `forecast-fingerprint`. Chips: **You** (tier + PBs across swim/bike/run + race history), **Capacity** (current CSS · FTP · VDOT; race-day projection lives in drill-down), **Response** (per-discipline adaptation ratios + race-readiness scores + signals feeding the model), **Training** (planned sessions/hours per discipline + per-discipline confidence), **Venue** (course factors aggregated per leg with factor bars and source caption), **Race-day** (transitions, durability vs threshold per discipline, OW swim count). Tap any chip to open a full-page drill-down with per-discipline rows, sparklines for CSS/FTP/VDOT history, factor breakdown bars, and a "missing factors lower confidence" caption when not enough data is present. Per-leg breakdown (swim/bike/run gaps vs today) stays on the main card; projected-fitness-markers and course-factors panels have moved off the main card into their respective drill-downs.
**Key files**: `src/ui/triathlon/race-forecast-fingerprint.ts` (chip builders + wire), `src/ui/triathlon/forecast-chip-detail-view.ts` (6 drill-down pages), wired from `src/ui/triathlon/race-forecast-card.ts` + `src/ui/triathlon/forecast-view.ts`
**Tests**: — (UI) ⚠️ Manual test only

### T9. Benchmark test prompts + confidence-tiered estimators + auto-detection
**What it does**: "Refine your benchmarks" cards pinned to the top of the triathlon plan view surface a CSS test (paired 400m + 200m, Smith-Norris) and an FTP test (20-min Coggan) when the auto-derived value is low-confidence. Cards self-suppress when the user's swim or ride history already supports a `'medium'`-or-better estimate, so users with rich power/swim data aren't nagged. Each card opens a compact result modal that writes the entered times directly into `triConfig.{swim,bike}` with `cssSource/ftpSource = 'user'` and confidence `'high'` so the launch refresh never overwrites a real test result. Stats and account benchmark cells show an inline "estimate · do the test" hint when the persisted CSS/FTP confidence is low/none. Wizard review CSS row hedges its caption based on the same tier.

**Auto-detection**: `looksLikeCssTest` in `tri-benchmarks-from-history.ts` heuristically flags swim activities matching the 400+200m protocol (600–1400m total, pace < 150s/100m, Z4+Z5 > 25% of time when HR is available). Activity detail shows a "Use as CSS test?" prompt when detected. `looksLikeFtpTest` similarly flags powered bike rides; activity detail shows an opt-in pill button ("Use as FTP test") on any ride with `deviceWatts === true` and avg ≥ 80W — defaults OFF to avoid false-positive auto-apply.

**Inline plan refresh**: Saving CSS or FTP from any entry path (benchmark card, activity detail) immediately calls `generateTriathlonPlan` via dynamic import so workout descriptions update without a reload.

**Workout card chips**: Cards show a `No CSS` or `No FTP` chip when the discipline's benchmark is missing, making it clear which targets are estimated.

**Key files**: `src/ui/triathlon/benchmark-tests-card.ts` (test prompts + inline regen), `src/calculations/tri-benchmarks-from-history.ts` (`CssEstimate.confidence`, `FtpEstimate.confidence`, `looksLikeCssTest`, `looksLikeFtpTest`), `src/ui/activity-detail.ts` (CSS/FTP prompts + opt-in pill), `src/ui/triathlon/workout-card.ts` (No CSS/FTP chips), `src/main.ts` (launch-time refresh writes confidence), `src/state/initialization.triathlon.ts` (wizard write-path), `src/ui/account-view.ts` (benchmarks grid), `src/ui/wizard/steps/review.ts` (CSS / FTP captions)
**Tests**: `src/calculations/tri-benchmarks-from-history.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (52 tests including 12 new confidence-tier scenarios)

### T10. Triathlon Progress detail page
**What it does**: Mirrors the running stats Progress detail in tri mode. Opens from a "Progress" card on the tri stats page. Range toggle (4w / 12w / All / Forecast). Charts: per-discipline CTL lines (daily-equivalent ÷7), three separate weekly km charts (swim / bike / run), one weekly TSS chart with three lines (swim/bike/run colours from `colours.ts`), FTP trend, CSS trend. All-time tile shows lifetime swim/bike/run km plus session counts. Forecast tab extends km + TSS using `triWorkouts` from the next 8 weeks (dashed continuation). FTP/CSS history is captured via `appendFtpSample` / `appendCssSample` whenever main.ts auto-derives a new value or a user enters one in onboarding — one entry per day, latest wins, 256-entry cap. Tracking-only data; no calculation reads from these arrays.
**Key files**: `src/ui/triathlon/progress-detail-view.ts`, `src/ui/triathlon/stats-view.ts` (Progress card + wiring), `src/calculations/tri-benchmark-history.ts` (history append helpers), `src/types/triathlon.ts` (`BikeBenchmarks.ftpHistory`, `SwimBenchmarks.cssHistory`), `src/main.ts` and `src/state/initialization.triathlon.ts` (history capture sites)
