---

## Fitness Calculations

---

### 1. VDOT Engine
**What it does**: Converts a race time (e.g. 5K in 22:00) into a single fitness number called VDOT. Higher VDOT = fitter runner. `s.v` is the engine VDOT: it sizes the plan (ability band, volume, long runs) and the forecast. Training paces come from the LT pace `s.lt` whenever it is set (`gp(vdot, lt)`, feature 2); VDOT sets them only when no LT exists.

`currentVDOT = s.v + sum(wk.wkGain) + s.rpeAdj + (s.physioAdj || 0)`

`physioAdj` is clamped to `Math.max(-5.0, rawAdj)` at all write sites to prevent stale LT data from making VDOT implausibly low. A `vdotHistory` array (last 20 entries, `{week, vdot, date}`) is appended on every VDOT-changing event (RPE rating, LT auto-update, week advance, manual fitness update, benchmark).

**VDOT-to-time inversion converges (2026-10-12, `33b635b`, SCIENCE_LOG §DD)**: `vt` and `predictFromVO2` bisect to their 50-iteration cap, so race time is continuous in VDOT (before, `vt` stopped at a 0.05 VDOT error and stepped up to 15 s at the marathon). Tests ✅ `vdot.test.ts` (cv(vt(v)) = v to 9 digits, 0.001 VDOT steps move the marathon strictly), `predictions.test.ts`.

**Key files**: `src/calculations/vdot.ts`, `src/ui/events.ts` (physioAdj + vdotHistory), `src/data/physiologySync.ts` (LT sanity check)
**Tests**: `src/calculations/vdot.test.ts` — ✅ Passing

---

### 2. Pace Generator
**What it does**: Turns the LT pace (or, without one, a VDOT score) into five training pace zones: easy, threshold, interval, marathon, and recovery. Every workout description references these paces.

**LT takes precedence**: `gp(vdot, lt)` ignores VDOT whenever an LT pace is set. Each zone is then a fixed ratio of LT (easy LT × 1.20, marathon × 1.05, threshold LT, interval × 0.93, repetition × 0.88). The renderer, `initializeSimulator` and the triathlon run-leg pace (`triRunEasyPaceSec`) all pass `s.lt`. `recomputeLT` (`src/data/ltSync.ts:173`) sets `s.lt` from an LT override, a Garmin LT reading, or a weighted blend of sustained-effort, PB critical-speed and Daniels estimates (`lt-derivation.ts:622-645`). `getPhysiologicalVdot` (`physiological-vdot.ts:385`) supplies only the Daniels input; its tiers, in order: a manual VO2max override, the Mosaic running estimate, a race anchor, a fresh watch VO2max, a medium-or-better HR fit, an observed LT, the PB median and, last, `s.v` ('tanda-fallback', `:523-533`). So a change to the engine VDOT moves the plan shape and the forecast but not the paces of a runner with any of the first seven. A runner with none of them gets the Daniels input from `s.v`, and `s.lt` and the paces follow the engine VDOT one refresh behind, unless a Garmin LT reading or an LT override sets `s.lt`. For a triathlete the Daniels input is capped since 2026-10-09 (`resolveLtDanielsInput`, `physiological-vdot.ts:769`, SCIENCE_LOG §DA): when the chain value is the watch or the engine fallback, it is held at the running-evidence ceiling (race anchor, PB capacity, medium-or-better HR fit, a synced race effort) or, with none, at the run skill VDOT. 70.3, run skill 3, watch 62: `s.lt` 288 s/km and easy runs at 5:46/km (before: 220 s/km and 4:24/km). ISSUE-372 has a note; not marked fixed.

**Training paces follow the LT (2026-10-12, `3bb40e3`, §DB)**: `s.pac` is a cache of `gp(getEffectiveVdot(s), s.lt)`, kept by one function, `syncTrainingPaces` (`paces.ts:54`), at every exit of `refreshBlendedFitness` (onboarding in every mode except cycling, and every launch), at every model write of `s.lt` (watch, activity sync, override) and in `refreshVO2Estimates`. With an LT the threshold pace is the LT exactly, so the LT page headline and its Training Paces Threshold card read one number. Before, triathlon, HYROX and Just-Track onboarding left the store default (Threshold 5:00/km beside an LT of 4:43 to 5:45) until the next launch. Cycling syncs at its first launch; no cycling surface shows the training paces.

**Model paces display to the nearest second (2026-10-12, `ced8427`, §DD)**: `s.lt` is stored unrounded (it drifts as PBs age) and every model pace (the LT, `s.pac`, description paces, HYROX and triathlon run paces) displays through one helper, `displayPaceSec` / `fpModel` / `fmtModelPaceKm` (`src/utils/format.ts:64-87`). A mile pace converts the whole-second km pace and carries the minute, so a pace card and a session description agree in either unit and no pace reads ":60". Actual-activity paces keep their own formatters.

**Key file**: `src/calculations/paces.ts`, `src/utils/format.ts`
**Tests**: `src/calculations/paces.test.ts` — ✅ Passing; `src/state/training-paces-follow-lt.test.ts` ✅ (real triathlon, HYROX, Just-Track and running initializers plus the launch order); `src/ui/model-pace-display.test.ts` ✅; `src/ui/lt-display-rounding.test.ts` ✅; `src/calculations/tri-run-lt-cap.test.ts` ✅. ❌ Not device-verified.

---

### 3. Race Time Predictor
**What it does**: Forecasts your finish time for race day based on current fitness, weeks of training remaining, runner type, and historical data. Shows an "initial baseline" and a "live forecast" that updates as you complete workouts.

The blended prediction (`refreshBlendedFitness`) combines Tanda (2011) marathon regression, VDOT-from-PBs, LT pace, and VO2max into a single weighted race time. Confidence scales with how many recent runs fed the blend. The blended effective VDOT is written back to `s.v` so plan generation and pace derivation always use the live value (except in taper/deload weeks where `s.v` is held — Mujika & Padilla 2000). `getEffectiveVdot(s)` layers RPE and physio adjustments on top.

**Onboarding anchor parity (2026-06-04, §BN)**: `initializeSimulator` applies the same long-run durability cap the refresher applies (blend → cap → VDOT) before seeding `blendedRaceTimeSec` / `initialBaseline` / forecast anchor, and stores `s.runDurability` from day 1 — previously a thin-data onboarding (single typed PB, empty scrape) showed the raw Riegel extrapolation until the first backfill silently slowed it. The weekly-km signal feeding the blend is resolved by `resolveWeeklyKmSignal` (`prediction-inputs.ts`): history-derived when the 8-week window has coverage, falling back to the user's typed manual-entry volume (`detectedWeeklyKm`, which now survives a zero-row Strava aggregation and sets `weeklyVolumeConfirmed` so it also seeds the plan's starting `wkm`). Tests ✅ `initialization.durability.test.ts`, `prediction-inputs.test.ts`, `initialization.volume.test.ts`.

**Accumulated time-on-feet softens durability (2026-06-17, §AL.4)**: the long-run durability cap originally penalised only on the single *peak* long run. It now also reads `qualifyingLongRunCount` (runs ≥ 70% of the distance threshold in the 12-week window) and *softens* the peak-shortfall penalty by `(1 − consistencyCredit)`, `consistencyCredit = min(1, count / 8)` (target ≈ one long run every 10 days, Pfitzinger). Softening only ever lowers the penalty, and is self-consistent (a short-peak, sparse runner can't qualify, so keeps the full penalty). Surfaced two ways: the Forecast **Durability** chip drill-down (`pageDurability`) gains a "Long runs (time on feet)" bar + count-vs-target row + a context line; the **injury-risk** view appends a one-line note when long-run consistency sits below half target (running mode, once load data exists). Running only — tri durability uses a different model (`durability-cap.ts`). Test ✅ `run-durability.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

**Single canonical model** (refactored 2026-05-05): "Forecast finish" (end-of-plan projection) and "Current Race Estimate" (today's predicted race time) now share a single anchor. `calculateLiveForecast` and `calculateForecast` accept an optional `blendedAnchorSec`; when supplied, the horizon model's VDOT gain is converted to a seconds delta via Daniels' table on both ends and subtracted from the blend's view of today. Prior to this fix, the headline was bare `tv(currentVdot+gain, dist)` while "Current Race Estimate" was the `blendPredictions` blend — two different models on different scales that could disagree by minutes (a 3:12 marathoner saw a "Slightly behind" pill at Week 1 with zero training done because the bare-VDOT forecast was 3:21). The "Slightly behind / On track" pill on the stats card was killed at the same time — after the fix, `forecastTime ≤ blendedToday ≤ initialBaseline` whenever the horizon predicts any positive gain, so the pill carried no information.

**Course-factor adjustment** (added 2026-05-05): when the user has selected a known race, the predictor layers race-specific factors on top of the raw VDOT prediction — climate (Ely 2007), altitude (Bonetti 2009), and elevation gain (Minetti 2002). The adjusted finish becomes the headline number; the raw fitness pace and per-factor delta are shown beneath. Pre-loaded course profiles cover Berlin, Boston, NYC, Chicago, London, Tokyo, Paris and 18+ other major marathons / 17 halfs (`src/data/marathon-course-profiles.ts`). `s.forecastTimeAdjusted` and `s.forecastCourseFactors` populate alongside `s.forecastTime`.

**Goal time + Monte Carlo chance (running)**: the user sets a race-day target (`s.raceGoalTimeSec`) and the app shows the live **chance of beating it** via `runMarathonForecastSim(s, 2000, goalSec)` → `goalProbability`, with a tone/label ladder (`liveTone`/`liveLabel` in `goal-time-overlay.ts`: Looks light ≥95% / Realistic ≥70% / Honest stretch ≥40% / Ambitious ≥15% / Aggressive). Two entry points: (a) post-onboarding slider overlay on the Race Forecast page (`openRunGoalOverlay`); (b) **onboarding plan-ready screen** (`plan-preview-v2.ts`, added 2026-06-01) — a "Do you have a goal time?" button opens an overlay with a goal slider, an "Add quality sessions" stepper, **and (added 2026-06-02) an "Add strength sessions" stepper** — all re-running the sim live so predicted finish + chance slide together. Strength sessions move the forecast via the running-economy projection (§BC / Training Horizon Model); both steppers feed one combined forecast and the same Monte Carlo chance. Saving reframes the hero to "TARGET TIME · X% chance · On current plan {forecast}". Adding sessions there is real: `bumpSessionsAndGymAndRegen()` raises the onboarding `runsPerWeek` (cap 7) and `gymSessionsPerWeek` (cap 3) and re-runs `initializeSimulator` once for both (the single-lever `bumpSessionsAndRegen()` still serves the auto "MILESTONE NEARBY → Target it" popup).

**Coach contract (all 3 plan modes, added + redesigned 2026-06-15)**: a "here's how your plan works" beat that sits as the last step before entering the app, after the plan exists — running `plan-preview-v2 → coach-contract → workout-preview → main-view`; triathlon `tri-plan-forecast → coach-contract → main-view`; HYROX `initializing → coach-contract → main-view`. (Cycling and just-track skip this step entirely — straight to `main-view`.) Coach voice (a deliberate first-person departure from the consultant default). The CTA calls `nextStep()` so the controller routes per mode; the plan-ready button reads "See how it works", the coach button "See your sessions" (running) / "Start training" (tri/HYROX). Background uses the full page-flair system (ring background + atmosphere + sun glint) to match the other wizard pages. **Periodisation fixes (2026-06-16, SCIENCE_LOG §CA):** the weekly-load chart now crests in the peak phase for short plans too (phase-aware variant selection in the engine, not a chart change), continuous plans climb cycle-over-cycle instead of looping flat, and the "how your sessions shift by phase" bars weight aerobic-vs-quality by LOAD (not session count) so low-frequency plans show the shift instead of a flat 50/50.

**Premium redesign (2026-06-15)** — restrained palette, one strong data-derived visual per idea, premium features SHOWN (not listed) via faithful mini-mockups of the real UI:
- **RUNNING** (from `derivePlanShape()`): (1) an **editorial single-accent load arc** — phases as labelled baseline regions + a peak marker, honest TSS currency; (2) a **per-phase aerobic-vs-quality session shift** (Base → Build → Peak → Taper, two colours) carrying the real **runner-type bias** (`applyRunnerTypeBias`); (3) a **cross-training** card showing ALL recurring sports with REAL `computeUniversalLoad` numbers, honest framing + the impromptu-session connection.
- **TRIATHLON** (from the stored `triWorkouts`): a **load ramp stacked by discipline** (swim/bike/run, canonical tri colours, per-week aerobic+anaerobic) + a **"your three engines"** card (FTP / CSS / run threshold; only rows with real data render).
- **HYROX** (same per-week derivation): a **run-vs-stations** stacked load ramp + a **weakest-link** card (`predictHyroxRace` + `topWeakestLinks` + `STATION_DISPLAY`; renders only when a projection exists). Removed the confusing "Compromised running" swap option.
- **Shared "Built to flex around you" showcase** (all modes, mode-aware copy): a **readiness ring** (canonical glass ring + `readinessColorStops`, score 52 = Manage Load easing today's hard session), a **touchable Adjust slider** (real `m-slider-glass`, live-updates distance/time at the athlete's real easy pace via `wireAdjustSlider`), a session **swap**, the weekly **rebalance** (anchored on impromptu cross-training), and the injury **Recovery mode** banner. Rebalance copy says "runs" in running mode, "sessions" in tri/HYROX.

**Personalised from real data** — `commonKnowFragments()` gates shared clauses on real signals (`ctlBaseline ≥ 20`, `hrCalibratedVdot.confidence !== 'none'`, recurring sports, `commuteConfig.enabled`); absent signals fall back to honest generic phrasing. **Shared source**: `src/calculations/plan-shape.ts` (`plannedWeekKm` / `parseDistanceKm` / `derivePlanShape` / `planPhaseSegments` / `deriveDisciplineLoad`); the same derivations feed the Stats "Training load across your plan" card (see Running Stats page below), so the onboarding arc and the Stats arc can never diverge. **Tests**: `coach-contract.test.ts` (5, jsdom — all three modes render). ⚠️ Not device-verified; live screenshots pending Playwright reconnect; copy not final-reviewed. **Key files**: `src/ui/wizard/steps/coach-contract.ts`, `src/calculations/plan-shape.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/controller.ts`.

**Goal time + add-training (triathlon, added 2026-06-02)**: the triathlon parity of the running onboarding control, on the `tri-plan-forecast` step (see T1). Same chance ladder (`liveTone`/`liveLabel`, now exported from `triathlon/goal-time-overlay.ts`) and the same `triConfig.targetFinishTimeSec` goal field used by the post-onboarding Forecast tab. The "add sessions" lever is **per discipline** (swim / bike / run) rather than a single counter, because triathlon's plan is hours-budget-driven with no integer session count — each +1 adds a discipline's representative session length and regenerates the plan in memory via `forecastTriWithDelta` (clone → `generateTriathlonPlan` → `predictTriathlonRace`), with the goal chance from `runTriForecastSim` on the same clone. Committed for real on Continue. SCIENCE_LOG §AZ.

Three surfaces expose the prediction. (1) **Home race-forecast card** (race mode only): distance label, predicted finish time (course-adjusted when applicable), target time, and signed delta vs target. Tap opens the full-page forecast view. *Not rendered (found 2026-10-12, `6a0ddc8`): `getHomeHTML` never calls `buildRaceForecastCard` and its click handler targets an element that is never created, so Home shows no forecast number in any mode (ISSUE-394).* (2) **Race forecast full-page** (`src/ui/race-forecast-view.ts`): hero ring, Started/Now/Forecast stat row, "Where you sit" population card, **forecast fingerprint card** — 5 glass chips in a row (You / Capacity / Response / Training / Venue) showing how each factor cluster feeds the prediction. Training chip uses real `garminActuals` km (4-week trailing average) with `detectedWeeklyKm` as pre-plan fallback — never the runs-per-week lookup. Response chip is gated on ≥ 4 vdotHistory samples and shows "Building signal" below that. **Every chip is tappable and opens a full detail page** (`src/ui/forecast-chip-detail-view.ts`, added 2026-05-19) — tab-style chrome with back button, sky atmosphere, sun glint, and a visual-first body (VDOT sparkline on Capacity, VDOT history chart + adaptation bar on Response, 8-week km bar chart on Training, per-factor bars on Venue, PB list on You). Below the fingerprint: course-factors panel with itemised breakdown (Berlin → "Climate cool: −0s, Elevation +60m: +12s"), line chart of race-time progression with dashed projection. (3) **Plan race-prediction row** shows Initial · Forecast with the signed delta subline. (4) **Stats race-estimate table** repeats the signed delta inline.

**No-target-race forecast (added 2026-06-01)**: when there is no goal event (`continuousMode`, on-demand, or just-track with PBs, or any user whose `s.rd`/`s.initialBaseline` is unset), the Forecast tab no longer bounces back to Home. It now renders a **standard-distance prediction view** (`getStandardForecastHTML` in `race-forecast-view.ts`) — a 2×2 grid of live 5K / 10K / half / marathon finish times from current fitness, plus the **racing-shoes segmented toggle**. Toggling Standard ↔ Supershoes re-renders all four tiles in place (no full reload), each moving by the flat 3% carbon boost. Every tile comes from one canonical helper, `predictStandardDistances` in `blended-fitness.ts`, which runs the *same* pipeline as the targeted blend (calibration bias → long-run durability cap → shoe boost) so a distance shown here equals what the targeted forecast would show if it were the goal. **Shared show/hide gate (2026-06-03)**: the grid shows iff `hasRunningFitnessSignal(s)` (`physiological-vdot.ts`) — the *same* predicate the Stats "VO2 Max" bar gates on, so a thin-data athlete can never see a VO2 bar on Stats but "No predictions yet" here. To honour that wider gate, `buildStandardPredictionContext` now gates on the signal (not on PBs / `trackOnly`), and `predictDistanceStaged` falls back to predicting from the resolved VDOT alone (`predictFromVO2`, the inverse Daniels model) when the weighted blend has no PB/LT/recent/device/HR input — so a track-only athlete with a real VDOT but no PBs now gets predictions. Empty state appears only when there is genuinely no running-fitness signal (`s.v ≤ 0` and no tier) and offers an "Import run history" CTA. Rationale in SCIENCE_LOG §BF.

**Per-distance drill-down (`renderStdDistanceDetail` in `race-forecast-view.ts`)**: tapping a tile opens a detail page with (1) a hero showing the adjusted predicted finish plus its **target pace** (even pace for that time, unit-aware — defined at every distance, unlike a split-by-split plan), (2) a **predicted-time-over-time trend** (`buildPredictedTimeTrendChart`, inverted so faster reads as up) reconstructed from `vdotHistory` via `tv(vdot, distKm)` — the raw fitness trajectory, mirroring how the targeted forecast separates trajectory from the adjusted headline. The headline and delta are **finish-time formatted** (e.g. `18:27`, `−0:12 since first sample` / `No change yet` when flat), not raw seconds — a dedicated time wrapper, parallel to the LT/CSS/CP pace wrappers, replaced the raw-number `buildBenchmarkTrendChart` headline that previously showed `1107.1`. When the user has a plan to project along (`s.rw > 0` + a VDOT), this card instead becomes a **forward projection** ("Projected {dist} time if you hold the plan"), rendered with the **liquid-glass sparkline treatment** (glassy card, vertical gradient fill, layered glow stroke, glowing dot+halo marker on the projected endpoint — same recipe as the marathon population card). A **dashed** curve runs from **Now** to a selectable **4 / 8 / 12-week horizon** (`buildForecastTrajectoryChart`, segmented selector re-renders in place); both endpoints are labelled with their times (Now {hero} → +N wk {projected}) so the direction of change is unambiguous. The projected series comes from `getStandardForecastProjection` — `applyTrainingHorizonAdjustment` (the targeted forecast's VDOT-gain model) sampled per week with `taper_weeks: 0` (no race to taper into) and **plan-prescribed volume** (`getPlanPrescribedMeanWeeklyKm`, the same dose the targeted forecast uses) — with the **gain floored at 0** (holding a plan is at minimum maintenance; never project getting slower). Then **scaled multiplicatively so "Now" equals the adjusted hero** the user already sees (not a raw-VDOT time on a different scale). Headline shows the projected finish + improvement ("19:14 · −9s in 12 weeks"). The dose + floor fix the earlier bug where half/marathon projected getting *slower* with a week-1 cliff (recent-km dose tripped the model's volume-gated undertrain penalty); the curve is now monotonic-improving for every user and distance. The historical-only trend (gated behind ≥3 points / ≥7 days) is the fallback when there is no plan to project along (a just-track user with no sessions). Science in SCIENCE_LOG §BA. (3) a **"How we reach {hero}" waterfall** decomposing the adjusted finish into ordered stages — Capacity (the blend) → Race calibration → Durability → Supershoes → **Predicted finish** — each row showing the running cumulative time and the signed delta it applied (slower = red, faster = green). Steps under 1s are hidden; the final row equals the hero because every stage reads `detail.stages` (`StandardPredictionStages`) from one `predictDistanceStaged` call shared with the grid, so the waterfall reconciles to the headline by construction (CLAUDE.md: two numbers, one source). The **Durability** row is named per distance — `long-run shortfall` for half/marathon, `aerobic durability` for 5K/10K (a short race's deficit is economy, not a missing long run) — and carries a one-line hint: when no run of the threshold duration is on record it says the endurance base is unproven (the no-history case that maxes the penalty), otherwise it names the threshold the longest recent run fell under. (4) a **"What feeds your capacity" card** listing the raw signals behind the Capacity row: Aerobic capacity (VDOT), Lactate threshold pace, Training volume (km/wk), Race calibration status. (5) the **Standard/Supershoes toggle** (same `buildStdShoesCard` as the grid) re-renders the detail in place so hero, pace, and waterfall all move by the 3% boost. Threshold seconds + longest-run seconds are surfaced on `StandardForecastDetail` (`durabilityThresholdSec`, `durabilityLongestRunSec`) so the durability copy is honest rather than implying a measurement that didn't happen. Data assembled by `getStandardForecastDetail(s, dist)` in `blended-fitness.ts`; the grid and the drill-down share `buildStandardPredictionContext` + `predictDistanceStaged`, so hero = grid tile = waterfall total. **History fix**: `refreshBlendedFitness` records a `vdotHistory` point via `recordVdotHistoryPoint(s)` even when no race distance is set, so the trend fills in for just-training users. Tests in `blended-fitness.test.ts` (24: monotonic ordering, 0.97 shoe ratio, track-only-with-VDOT-predicts + no-running-signal null guard, hero=grid, trend reconstruction, no-race history recording, VO2-override flow-through, waterfall-stage reconciliation, supershoes-stage shift, projection point-count + monotonic-improvement + longer-horizon-faster + null gating), plus the canonical-gate helper tests in `physiological-vdot.test.ts` (`resolveRunningVdot` / `hasRunningFitnessSignal`).

**Race-day durability from the plan's own long runs (2026-10-07, SCIENCE_LOG §CU)**: the race-day forecast applies the long-run durability cap (same thresholds and penalties as today's cap) to the runs the plan prescribes in the 12 weeks ending with race week, plus logged runs for any pre-plan weeks in that window (`raceDayPlanDurability`, `src/calculations/race-day-durability.ts`). A plan whose long runs stay under the distance threshold carries the penalty; one that builds to it does not. Before, the race-day forecast assumed every plan delivered the threshold long run. Every running forecast producer passes the multiplier to `calculateLiveForecast` (refresh, onboarding preview, `rate()`, renderer, strength benefit, debug) and the onboarding init applies the same multiplier to `s.forecastTime`, `rate()` anchors on the published peak-volume anchor, and `s.raceDayDurability` stores the applied result, which Forecast → Durability shows: the banner states the plan's longest run before race day and the penalty, and the row "Applied to race-day forecast" shows the same percentage. The Durability page, the plan preview and the Forecast page show a plain statement ("The longest run before race day is about H:MM, short of the 2:45 marathon-readiness long run. The forecast assumes long-run endurance the plan does not build, so the finish time is optimistic."; `raceDayEnduranceGapText` with `enduranceGapPlanContext`) in two cases: (a) any marathon plan whose longest run before race day stays under 1:55, where the row also reads "Not estimable: long runs too short" (`raceDayEnduranceBeyondModel`); (b) a marathon sharpening block for a beginner (experience level total beginner or beginner) or for a runner with no run in the 30 days before build, where the statement shows and the row keeps its percentage whenever the longest run is 1:55 or more. In both cases the statement replaces the banner's penalty sentence. Neither fires once the longest run reaches 2:45. Effect: marathon plans of low-baseline or 4-hour runners up to 3.8% slower, every half plan 0.5 to 2.3% slower, every 10K plan up to 1.4% slower, 5K and established marathon plans that build to 165 min unchanged. Open decisions in ISSUE-358. Tests ✅ `race-day-durability.test.ts`, `race-day-durability.forecast.test.ts`, `initialization.durability.test.ts`, `rate-forecast-durability.test.ts`, `durability-detail-endurance-gap.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

**Short plans and the race calendar in the forecast (2026-10-07/08, SCIENCE_LOG §CV, §CW)**: a sharpening block's volume anchor is its own peak pre-taper week (`plannedPeakWeeklyKm`). `planForecastWeeklyKm` (`blended-fitness.ts:466`) is the one forecast-volume reader for Home (`refreshBlendedFitness`), the onboarding preview hero (`plan-preview-v2.ts`), the dashboard recompute (`renderer.ts`), the legacy plan init (`events.ts` `init`), the strength-benefit line and `debugForecast`, so the preview hero equals the built plan's `s.forecastTime` (it was up to 383 s faster for a 6-week block at 15 km a week). The forecast's taper is the plan's (`planTaperWeeks`) and `TAPER_NOMINAL.marathon` is 2, so a 2-week marathon taper earns the full taper bonus. Every weeks-to-race input reads `planTrainingWeeks`, so a Monday to Wednesday race forecasts exactly like the Sunday before (its closing week holds no training). The goal-check and race-forecast buttons read "Add a run a week" instead of "Add a quality session" when one more run would be an easy run (`extraRunAddsQualitySession`). Known: Home's forecast after the first refresh can differ from the onboarding headline by 4 to 21 min (ISSUE-359). Tests ✅ `block-forecast-honesty.test.ts`, `forecast-taper.test.ts`, `closing-week-forecast.test.ts`, `plan-preview-forecast-parity.test.ts`, `initialization.sharpening.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

**Recent race effort (2026-10-08, SCIENCE_LOG §CY)**: the blend's recent-run predictor, the §BZ engine-floor slowness gate and the §BV demonstrated-PB floor read `computePredictionInputs().recentRun`, which is now the newest run in the 8-week window more than 15% faster than the window's median pace (`RACE_FILTER_RATIO` 0.85, `prediction-inputs.ts:200`); with none they fall back to the recent race typed at onboarding (`s.rec`). Before, it was the newest run of any effort, so one easy run read as a slow race and pulled the engine down: a 10K 45:00 runner (45.3) with easy runs at about 5:50/km (test seed, 4 runs and 35 km a week) read 37.3 at onboarding and 39.3 after a refresh and deloaded every 3rd week; now 45.3 and every 4th. Easy runs feed weekly volume, Tanda's training pace and the HR calibration. The engine VDOT moves the ability band, plan volume and long runs, the forecast and the VDOT shown; it moves paces only for a runner whose LT takes its Daniels input from `s.v`, one with no VO2max override, PB, fresh watch reading, medium-or-better HR fit, race anchor, Mosaic estimate or observed LT (feature 2). The forecast breakdown's Recent run row reads "(from latest race effort)". Triathlon cold-start cap: with no running PB, race anchor, synced race effort or medium-or-better HR fit, the run engine stays at the run skill rating, and easy runs do not lift it (`blended-fitness.ts:717`). Known limitations, pinned as tests: the race test is pace-only, so tempo sessions and tempo-effort parkruns count as races and lower VDOT (ISSUE-374); a stale PB is not corrected by training runs or the HR fit (ISSUE-373); a typed recent race applies to synced athletes and never ages (ISSUE-377); a synced race effort lifts the triathlete cap and lets the watch number back in (ISSUE-376); triathlon run paces still follow the uncapped watch through `s.lt` (ISSUE-372). Tests ⚠️ (passing, with known-limitation pins): `src/calculations/prediction-inputs.test.ts`, `src/state/initialization.easy-runs-vdot.test.ts`.

**Race test with heart rate (2026-10-09/11, `ff2d980`, `59a1675`, `34e8b21`, SCIENCE_LOG §CY update)**: a run with an average HR, for an athlete with HR zones, fills the recent-run slot only when it also averages at or above the middle of Z4, `(z4.min + z4.max) / 2` (`raceEffortHrFloorBpm`, `heart-rate.ts:137`; Karvonen 190 / 50: 169 bpm). Tanda's P stays pace-filtered. A 10K 45:00 runner with a 5 km tempo at HR 165 or 168 holds 45.3 (pace-only: 40.3). Runs without HR keep the pace-only test, so ISSUE-374 now applies to them only; a half marathon averaging in the lower half of Z4 is a known false negative. Since `014148c` a synced race effort is in the triathlete running-evidence ceiling, and triathlon run paces follow the capped figure (feature 2; notes on ISSUE-372 and ISSUE-376). Tests ✅ `prediction-inputs.test.ts`, `initialization.easy-runs-vdot.test.ts` (known-limitation pins remain for runs without HR and for typed stale PBs).

**PB ageing (2026-10-09/11, SCIENCE_LOG §DC)**: PBs display as entered or synced; inside the model a dated PB (Strava or Apple auto-fill) weighs `exp(−years × ln 2 / 3)`: 1 year 0.79, 2 years 0.63, 4 years 0.40. Typed PBs have no date and never age. The weight applies only with a measured weekly volume (synced or typed, `modelPbAgeWeightsIfMeasured`), to PB capacity (an interpolated weighted median), the §BZ engine floor (pulled toward a medium-or-better HR fit), the blend's PB weight, the same-distance PB floor and the LT's PB inputs. No number steps on a PB's anniversary: forecasts move under 1 s a day as a PB ages. Tests ✅ `pb-age-weight.test.ts`, `pb-age-continuity.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

**PB-recency factor is continuous (2026-10-12, SCIENCE_LOG §DE)**: the low-volume marathon-specificity penalty's PB-recency scaling (and the triathlon legs') is `pbRecencyPenaltyFactor`: 0.5 through the first year, linear to 0.75 at two years and to the full penalty at three, never above the old 365/730-day bands. The old band edges moved a low-volume marathon about 159 s overnight. Tests ✅ `race-readiness.test.ts`, `pb-age-continuity.test.ts`.

**One forecast at onboarding and launch (2026-10-11/12, SCIENCE_LOG §DB)**: `initializeSimulator` ends with the launch refresh, and the plan preview reads `runningForecastForSessions`, the computation the refresh stores as `s.forecastTime`, so the preview hero equals Home's first forecast (before, a marathon PB 3:15 at 2.02 years with a typed 40 km read 3:09:36 in the preview and 3:16:25 after launch). Triathlon, HYROX and Just-Track onboarding end with the same refresh; cycling does not. Note added to ISSUE-359. Tests ✅ `one-forecast-mirror.test.ts`, `just-track-one-forecast.test.ts`, `hyrox-init-resolved-fitness.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

**Every "if racing today" surface reads one computation (2026-10-11/12, `bdeedf3`, `fa29053`)**: the Stats "Current Race Estimates" card and the session generator's Time Trial read `predictStandardDistances`, the Forecast's standard-distance predictions (PB age weights, the HR-tested recent race, the measured weekly km, calibration, the durability cap). Before, a half PB 1:32 three years old with a typed 40 km read a 3:40:14 marathon on Stats against 3:21:49 on the Forecast. A triathlon cold start holds these predictions at the run skill cap. Tests ✅ `pb-age-surfaces.test.ts`, `tri-time-trial-cap.test.ts`.

**PB-age caption (2026-10-09 to 12, `9df38ae`, §DC)**: "Older PBs carry less weight in ..." under the Stats race estimates, the running Forecast tab header, the no-race Forecast footnote, the triathlon Forecast and the HYROX run-pace note, only when PB ageing moves a number that surface displays by at least 1 s against the unaged computation (`pbAgeCaptionText`, `src/ui/pb-age-caption.ts:68`). Muted 11px caption. Not on Home. Tests ✅ `pb-age-caption.test.ts`, `pb-age-surfaces.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

### 4. Training Horizon Model
**What it does**: Limits how fast your VDOT can realistically grow over time — so the app doesn't predict unrealistic improvements. Takes into account starting fitness, weeks available, runner type, **and weekly training volume**. A 10 h/wk runner now sees a meaningfully larger predicted gain than a 3 h/wk runner with the same session count, because raw `sessions_per_week` is converted into `effective_sessions` via a dose multiplier (`actual_km_per_session ÷ ref_km_per_session`, clamped to [0.5, 1.3]). Reference km-per-session per distance comes from Daniels/Pfitzinger intermediate plans (`REF_KM_PER_SESSION` in `src/constants/training-params.ts`). Hours fallback (`HOURS_TO_KM_RATE = 10`) covers users with no run history. Same lever applies in triathlon — the run-leg adjuster passes `weekly_volume_km` to the same function.

**Strength → faster forecast (added 2026-06-02)**: planned strength sessions now contribute a projected running-economy gain to the forecast. Concurrent strength + plyometric work improves running economy (~2-4%) and race performance (~2-5%) over 8-12 weeks (Beattie & Blagrove 2018), so the horizon model adds `3% × (1 − e^(−weeks/10)) × min(sessions/2, 1)` to the projected improvement — a *future-projection* term (the strength in your plan will make you faster across the block), not a retroactive multiplier on current fitness (which would double-count the economy already baked into the current blend). Folded under the same `max_gain_cap_pct` clamp and VDOT-barrier guardrails as the run gain, so it can't push anyone through a time barrier. Gated on planned `gymSessionsPerWeek` (`s.gs`); `0` → no change for non-lifters. Onboarding's plan-preview shows a callout isolating the benefit ("Your N strength sessions are worth about X of the projected finish"). Magnitude (3% ceiling) signed off by Tristan; science in `SCIENCE_LOG.md → §BC`.

**Key files**: `src/calculations/training-horizon.ts`, `src/constants/training-params.ts`, `src/constants/strength-constants.ts`
**Tests**: `src/calculations/training-horizon.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (63 tests, incl. 7 volume-effect + 6 strength-contribution cases)

### 5. Fatigue Model
**What it does**: Models accumulated fatigue from training volume. Affects how much fitness gain you get each week — heavy weeks build fitness but also accumulate fatigue that reduces the gain from subsequent weeks.

**Key file**: `src/calculations/fatigue.ts`
**Tests**: `src/calculations/fatigue.test.ts` — ✅ Passing

---

### 6. Physiology Tracker
**What it does**: Tracks your lactate threshold (LT) pace and VO2max over the course of the plan, updating them based on workout feedback. These values feed into more precise pace targets and HR zones.

**Key file**: `src/calculations/physiology-tracker.ts`
**Tests**: `src/calculations/physiology-tracker.test.ts` — ✅ Passing

---

### 6a. Effort-Calibrated VDOT (per-segment HR regression)
**What it does**: Estimates current running VDOT from recent training by fitting a weighted linear regression of pace on %HRR (Swain & Leutholtz 1997 %HRR ≈ %VO2R), then mapping the projected pace at vVO2max to VDOT via Daniels' tables. Per-segment input (per-km from Strava `splits_metric`) means within-run variation — e.g. a tempo finish on a long run — feeds the regression as separate (pace, HR) points spanning the run's HRR range, instead of being averaged into one diluted point per run.

**Key files**: `src/calculations/effort-calibrated-vdot.ts`, `src/calculations/vo2-orchestrator.ts`, edge fn `supabase/functions/sync-strava-activities/index.ts` (per-km HR extraction)
**Tests**: `src/calculations/effort-calibrated-vdot.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (22 tests; 17 run-level + 5 segment-level)
**Science**: `docs/SCIENCE_LOG.md → Effort-Calibrated VDOT (2026-04-24)` + per-segment extension (2026-05-12)

---

### 6b. Lactate Threshold Derivation (own engine, decoupled from Garmin)
**What it does**: When Garmin's watch-side LT reading is missing, stale, or gated, the app derives its own LT pace + LTHR from three blended methods: Daniels T-pace from VDOT (88% vVO2max), Critical Speed from race-distance PBs (LT = 0.93 × CS), and empirical detection from sustained tempo efforts (20+ min runs, 85–92% HRmax band, decoupling <5%). Outlier guards exclude treadmill, hot weather, hilly runs, and unsteady pacing.

**State integration**: `recomputeLT(s)` runs after every physio + activity sync, and (2026-06-04, §BO) at onboarding finalize when the wizard supplied no measured LT — so `s.lt`/`ltSource`/`ltConfidence` are consistent from day 1. Priority chain: `s.ltOverride` > fresh Garmin reading (<60d) > blended derived. When Garmin and our derived value differ by >10s/km, a `s.ltSuggestion` is set so the LT detail page can prompt the user to choose; `s.lt` is not silently overwritten in that case. PBs feed the chain via `effectivePhysiologyPbs` (per-key fastest of `s.pbs` + in-progress wizard `s.onboarding.pbs`), so mid-wizard typed PBs are visible before finalize. The blended estimate is bounded by demonstrated performance: LT pace can never be slower than the average pace of a race PB longer than 60 minutes (clamped, provenance notes "Capped at your race-PB pace", confidence downgraded from high).

**Stats UI**: The LT metric detail page (Stats → LT card) shows the active value with provenance + confidence chip, a per-method breakdown (pace and weight contribution), the Garmin sparkline when present, the conflict-resolution prompt, and slider overrides for pace + LTHR with reset-to-derived.

**Workout pacing**: `s.lt` flows into `paces.gp(vdot, ltPace)` so threshold workouts and pace zones use the resolved value automatically; `syncTrainingPaces` refreshes the `s.pac` cache on every write of `s.lt` (feature 2).

**Unrounded LT and PB age (2026-10-09 to 12, SCIENCE_LOG §DC, §DD)**: `s.lt` keeps the resolved pace unrounded (`applyResolved`, `ltSync.ts:291`) and every display rounds it to the whole second, so a daily drift no longer ticks race times by tens of seconds. Under the measured-volume gate, critical speed's method weight is multiplied by the mean age weight of its PBs, and a dated long-race PB pulls the race-PB bound only its age weight of the way. Tests ✅ `pb-age-continuity.test.ts`, `lt-display-rounding.test.ts`.

**Key files**: `src/calculations/lt-derivation.ts`, `src/data/ltSync.ts`, `src/ui/stats-view.ts → buildLTMetricPage`
**Tests**: `src/calculations/lt-derivation.test.ts` — ✅ Passing (34 tests, incl. race-PB pace cap); finalize consistency in `src/state/initialization.physiology.test.ts`
**Science**: `docs/SCIENCE_LOG.md → Lactate Threshold Derivation` (Daniels, Jones & Vanhatalo, Nixon et al. 2021, Friel)

### 7. HR & Efficiency Scoring
**What it does**: When you log a workout with heart rate data, the app cross-checks your RPE (how hard it felt) against your HR to detect whether you're getting fitter, fatigued, or under cardiovascular stress. This nudges your future paces slightly up or down.

**Logic**: Low RPE + Low HR = efficiency signal (paces get a tiny bit faster). Low RPE + High HR = cardiovascular strain (dampens the easy-day signal). High RPE + High HR = struggle (slows future paces). High RPE + Low HR during intervals = central fatigue.

**Rated-session HR zones read the LT heart rate (2026-10-11, `9693de3`)**: the RPE + HR flow, the threshold-run LT estimate and the Z2 efficiency points build zones from `ratingHrProfile(s)` (`events.ts:449`: `s.ltHR`, then synced max and resting HR, then onboarding values, then age). They passed the LT pace before, so a 300 s/km LT became a 300 bpm LTHR and every HR read far below target. Test ✅ `src/ui/rate-hr-zones.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

**Key file**: `src/calculations/heart-rate.ts`
**Tests**: `src/calculations/efficiency.test.ts` — ✅ Passing

### 7b. HR Effort + Pace Adherence → Plan Engine (ISSUE-35 Build 1)
**What it does**: Every synced run gets two objective scores: (1) HR effort score — how hard it was relative to the target HR zone (from Strava HR data), and (2) pace adherence — how close actual pace was to target pace (`gp(s.v, s.lt)`: from the LT when `s.lt` is set, else from VDOT tables). These blend into the weekly `effortScore` alongside RPE, which the plan engine uses to scale future workout durations.

**Weighting**: Quality sessions (threshold, VO2, marathon pace) weight pace at 35% — missing pace targets on hard workouts is the strongest signal. Easy runs weight pace at only 15%. HR fills the gap between RPE and reality.

**User-facing**: Future weeks in the plan show an adaptive note explaining workouts will adjust, with context ("you missed pace targets on recent quality sessions").

**Key files**: `src/calculations/heart-rate.ts` (`computeHREffortScore`), `src/calculations/activity-matcher.ts` (`computePaceAdherence`, `getHREffort`, `getPaceAdherence`), `src/ui/events.ts` (blended effort score), `src/ui/plan-view.ts` (`buildAdaptiveNote`)
**Tests**: ⚠️ No dedicated tests yet — logic verified via existing 756 tests passing

### 7c. Workout Commentary — Coach's Notes (ISSUE-35 Build 3)
**What it does**: Every completed activity gets 2-3 sentences of coaching commentary on its detail screen. Rules-based — picks the most relevant insights from pace adherence, HR effort, HR drift, split patterns (negative split, late fade, evenness), and HR zone distribution.

**HR drift surfaces (added 2026-04-15)**:
- **Activity detail copy** (`workout-insight.ts`): drift >8% on easy/long runs (not quality) triggers a layman's explanation — heat, dehydration, fatigue, or pace too aggressive.
- **Pre-long-run nudge** (`daily-coach.ts → computeLongRunDriftNote`): when today is a long run and recent long runs have drifted, the coach suggests earlier fuelling and pace control. Surfaced under the coach message in Readiness view.
- **Easy-pace commentary** (`daily-coach.ts → detectEasyDriftPattern`): when easy runs over the last 3 weeks drift >5% on average, the week debrief adds a note that easy pace may be too close to aerobic threshold.
- **Aerobic Durability chart** (`stats-view.ts → buildDurabilityChart`): Stats → Progress card showing drift over last 12 weeks (easy/long only) with 4-session rolling mean and threshold bands.
- **Marathon fade-risk badge** (`stats-view.ts → computeMarathonFadeRisk`): Race Estimates card pill on the Marathon row — Low/Moderate/High based on long-run drift at goal MP.
- **Running Stats page** (2026-05-29 rebuild, `stats-view.ts → buildStatsSummary`): mirrors the triathlon stats opening. **Your Numbers card** (`buildRunningNumbersCard`): LT pace + VO2 Max as single-teal zone-position bars, each tapping straight into its metric detail page: **LT** → `buildLTMetricPage` (watch-history sparkline + override sliders); **VO2 Max** → the shared `buildVO2DetailPage` (`triathlon/benchmark-detail-pages.ts`), the same rich page triathlon uses (per-source breakdown, Mosaic/Watch/Manual source toggle, override slider, explainer; mode-agnostic, no `triConfig`). Back returns to the Stats opening. **Progress section** (`buildProgressSection`): a Recent-weeks summary card (last 5 weeks time/sessions/load, taps into the full Progress detail page), a **"Training load across your plan"** card (`renderPlanShapeCard`, `src/ui/plan-shape-chart.ts` — the onboarding plan-shape arc with a you-are-here marker; running load line, tri swim/bike/run stack, HYROX run-vs-stations; hidden for just-track), plus a running-load (CTL) trend chart. **Mint** page-flair background (was the blue `sky` palette). All zone bars, phase-timeline base/peak, sleep mid-tier, and HRV recoloured off blue/purple onto green/teal. The old Progress / Fitness / Readiness / Summary opening cards were removed — their content lives in the drilldowns. ⚠️ Built + tsc/lint/build clean; not yet device-verified.

**Tone**: Direct, coaching voice. Not robotic, not sycophantic. Praises good execution, flags issues with actionable context ("consider starting more conservatively"), explains how the plan adapts.

**Works for all activity types** — runs get pace/split/drift analysis, cross-training gets HR effort + load commentary.

---

### 7d. Per-rep interval analysis (run + bike) (2026-05-06)
**What it does**: For any structured interval session — `8×400m`, `6×800m`, `5×5min @ FTP`, `30×30s` — each rep is detected, scored against its target, and summarised on the activity-detail page with a one-line execution commentary. The same per-rep adherence drives the running effort blend (`events.ts`) and the bike effort multiplier (`effort-multiplier.triathlon.ts`) so progression next week reflects rep-level execution rather than whole-session averages.

**Two detection paths**:
1. **Strava `/laps`** — primary for run; clusters laps by pace and takes the fast cluster as reps. Uniform 1km/1mile auto-laps are rejected (CoV gate, since bike computers commonly auto-lap regardless of what the rider did).
2. **Stream-based fallback** — when laps are absent or auto-generated. Power stream is the *primary* signal for bike (more reliable than laps); pace stream is the fallback for run.

**Per-rep scoring**: `parseRepPrescription` extracts the rep pattern (`8×400m`, `5×5min`, etc.) from the workout description. Targets resolve via `gp()` for run (interval/threshold pace from VDOT or LT) and `BIKE_TARGET_IF × FTP` for bike. In-band tolerance: ±5% pace for run, per-session-type band for bike. Aggregate: `inBand`, `fadePct` (last-half vs first-half mean), `avgAdherence`. Power fade is sign-flipped so positive always = "got worse over the set".

**UI**: contextual rep block on `activity-detail.ts`, only renders when `repData` is present. Shows source ("Strava laps" / "Auto-detected"), per-rep table (rep#, distance, bar, in-band marker, HR, pace/watts), and a one-line commentary ("8/8 reps in target band, no fade — strong execution.", "First 4 held the band, last 4 faded 6.2%", "5/5 reps under target — undercooked set").

**DB**: new `garmin_activities.rep_data jsonb` column. Detection runs in the standalone edge-function path (one-time per new activity; cached results skip re-fetching).

**Key files**: `src/calculations/rep-detection.ts`, `src/calculations/rep-adherence.ts`, `src/types/state.ts` (`ActivityRep`, `ActivityRepData`), `supabase/functions/sync-strava-activities/index.ts`, `src/ui/activity-detail.ts`.
**Tests**: `rep-detection.test.ts` (12), `rep-adherence.test.ts` (22) — ✅ Passing.

**Key files**: `src/calculations/workout-insight.ts` (`generateWorkoutInsight`), `src/ui/activity-detail.ts` (renders "Coach's Notes" card)
**Tests**: ⚠️ No dedicated tests yet

## Training Plan

### 7d. On-Demand Mode (internally `trackOnly` / "Just-Track")
**Renamed 2026-06-01**: the user-facing label is now **"On demand"** (tile in `goals.ts`, focus option in `race-target.ts`), framed for athletes who want to train but have no event on the calendar. The internal flag stays `trackOnly` and code/comments still say "Just-Track". It is no longer tracking-only: On-demand users **generate sessions** (run / bike / swim / gym) via the session generator — see §15b. Self-correction note: an earlier read of this work assumed only running could be generated without benchmarks; in fact `generateBikeSession`/`generateSwimSession` take **optional** FTP/CSS and `generateGymWorkouts` needs neither, so all four disciplines work for a no-plan user.

**What it does**: Onboarding path for users with no training plan generated. The user picks the **"On demand"** tile on the mode picker (`goals.ts`), or the **"On demand"** option on the fitness focus picker (4th row, after Endurance / Speed / Balanced). Under the hood they get the same infrastructure as any non-event `continuousMode` user — one rolling week that extends on calendar advance — with plan generation suppressed and prescription UI hidden. Sync (Strava / Garmin / Apple), GPS recording, activity matching, physiology polling, readiness, CTL/ACWR all run unchanged. A prominent **"Generate a session"** card is the primary action on the On-demand home.

Upgrading to a plan later is a one-tap action from Home or Plan: `upgradeFromTrackOnly()` (`wizard/controller.ts:600`) flips the wizard's record (`onboarding.trackOnly`, `trainingFocus`, `currentStep`) and sets `hasCompletedOnboarding: false`, the flag the launch routing (`main.ts:992`) and the onboarding sex gate (`isTrimpSexPending`, `trimp-sex-gate.ts:111`) read. It leaves `s.trackOnly` and the weeks untouched, so accumulated CTL / synced activities / physiology are preserved, and relaunches the wizard at the goals step. `s.trackOnly` stays true until a plan init branch builds the plan (2026-10-06). Tapping Return to plan before that calls `restoreJustTrackIfNoPlanBuilt` (`controller.ts:650`), which writes back the Just-Track onboarding record (`JUST_TRACK_ONBOARDING`, the same field set the goals tile writes), so the user lands on Just-Track Home with no phantom running week and no leftover HYROX or cycling mode. Tests ✅ `upgrade-from-track-only-return.test.ts`. Open: Switch to Just-Track (`downgradeToTrackOnly`) keeps `trainingMode`, so triathlon, cycling and HYROX plans are rebuilt instead of switched (ISSUE-351).

**What's hidden in track-only mode**: today-workout card, Coach/Check-in buttons, race-forecast card, week progress display, Stats Progress card (plan adherence), phase labels everywhere.

**What's kept**: readiness ring (informational), weekly volume card, recent activity feed, Stats Fitness card (CTL/VDOT — derived from actuals).

**Daily load target**: `CTL / 7 × readinessMultiplier` (readiness 80+ → ×1.3, 60–79 → ×1.0, 40–59 → ×0.7, <40 → ×0.3). Today's actual TSS rendered with Gabbett-band colour — green ≤1.3× CTL, amber 1.3–1.5, red >1.5 (Gabbett 2016 injury-risk sweet spot). Suppressed below `ctlBaseline < 20` with a "sync more history" empty state. Full rationale in `docs/SCIENCE_LOG.md → "Just-Track Daily Load Target"`.

**Plan tab**: retrospective log only — current week's activity-by-day detail plus a rolling history list of prior weeks (distance / sessions / TSS per row). No forward planning. The This week card and the History rows read one list, `trackOnlyWeekLog` (`plan-view.ts:4042`), built on `collectWeekActivities`, so each activity counts once: a GPS run no longer shows a second undated row, and an accepted synced run no longer doubles km and the session count (2026-10-06). A generated session counts once it is done; pending synced items stay off the card, as they carry no load. Tests ✅ `plan-view-track-only-week-log.test.ts`. Home's Just-Track This week card still runs its own loop (ISSUE-351).

**Mark as Done and plan changes (2026-10-06/07)**: Mark as Done on a generated session stores its planned load as an in-app record (§20c), so Just-Track week TSS includes it; a km-only run uses the distance at easy pace for its duration. No running plan change (cross-training cuts, Tier-1 trim, ACWR Reduce, timing suggestions) runs in Just-Track (`hasRunningPlanWeek`), and Activity Review offers no plan slots. Open: cross-training overflow still opens the running suggestion modal with no adjustments (ISSUE-351).

**Week debrief**: auto-triggers on Sunday / Monday as usual, but shows `showTrackOnlyRetrospective` — compact this-vs-last-week deltas on distance, sessions, TSS, CTL, recovery average. No plan-adherence language.

**Wizard flow**: welcome → goals (pick "On demand" tile directly) → connect-data → review → initializing (short-circuits, skips race-target / schedule / physiology / runner-type / plan-preview) → main-view. The "On demand" tile sets `onboarding.trackOnly=true` and `initializeSimulator` takes the trackOnly branch.

**Fitness computed once at onboarding (2026-10-12, `2ad0cb8`, SCIENCE_LOG §DB)**: the trackOnly branch ends with the launch refresh (`refreshBlendedFitness`, after the archived activities move into the rolling week; without PBs it runs `recomputeLT` and stops before the blend, as at launch), so `s.v`, `s.lt`, the training paces and the Stats race estimates are the same before and after the first relaunch (10K 45:00: the Stats marathon read 3:52:33 at onboarding and 3:49:58 after). A typed weekly km sets no archetype and no weekly target in Just-Track (`typedVolumeShapesPlan`, 2026-10-11, `4991e58`). Tests ✅ `src/state/just-track-one-forecast.test.ts`, `src/state/typed-km-mode-gates.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

**Readiness + strain detail views**: opening the readiness ring or strain breakdown on track-only home renders without the plan-comparison section (`generateWeekWorkouts` gated on `!s.trackOnly`, and `coachingText` / status labels in strain-view adapt for trackOnly). The HRV / sleep / RHR composite still computes; `plannedDayTSS` stays 0.

**Account-view safety**: Change-Runner-Type button hidden for trackOnly. Reset-Plan relabels to "Reset tracking history". "Switch to tracking only" button in Advanced lets plan-mode users opt into track mode without losing CTL/history. "Recurring activities" row in Training section lets users remove sports post-onboarding.

**Plan → track transition**: race-complete banner on home when `selectedMarathon.date < today` offers a one-tap switch. `downgradeToTrackOnly()` in `wizard/controller.ts` is the underlying function (inverse of `upgradeFromTrackOnly`). Both mode flips go through the `initializing` step with a mode-change guard so `initializeSimulator` re-runs cleanly.

**Polish (2026-04-24)**: First-launch orientation line under hero when no data. Sleep-log affordance under readiness ring. "Create a plan" demoted from full-width button to muted link. Daily-target card: "target" → "sustainable" to de-prescribe. Unified empty-state copy: "Connect Strava or record a run" across all cards. Record tab copy branches: "Record a run" instead of "Just Run — we'll fit it into your plan".

**Key files**: `src/state/initialization.ts` (trackOnly branch), `src/ui/welcome-back.ts` (calendar extension), `src/ui/home-view.ts` (`getTrackOnlyHomeHTML`, `buildTrackOnlyDailyTarget`, "Tracking" pill), `src/ui/plan-view.ts` (`getTrackOnlyPlanHTML`), `src/ui/stats-view.ts` (track-only summary branch), `src/ui/readiness-view.ts` + `src/ui/strain-view.ts` (trackOnly guards on `generateWeekWorkouts`), `src/ui/account-view.ts` (button guards), `src/ui/events.ts` (`showResetModal` copy), `src/ui/week-debrief.ts` (`showTrackOnlyRetrospective`), `src/ui/wizard/controller.ts` (`upgradeFromTrackOnly`, trackOnly branches in `nextStep()`).
**State**: `s.trackOnly: boolean`, `s.onboarding.trackOnly: boolean`, `TrainingFocus` includes `'track'`.
**Tests**: Manual — typecheck passes, pre-existing test suite unchanged. End-to-end smoke path documented in `.claude/plans/dazzling-knitting-sun.md` — not yet browser-tested.

### 7d. Running Onboarding — Time Budget + Workout Preview

**What it does**: Two additions to the running onboarding wizard, ported from triathlon/cycling learnings:

1. **~~Weekly time budget~~ (removed 2026-06-01)**: The "Total training time per week" card (peak hours/week slider + Mon–Fri/weekend split) was cut from the schedule step. It manufactured a "Scheduled vs Target → fill the gap" homework framing while run lengths were always set by race + ability + phase, not the slider — the card had to apologise for itself ("Run lengths follow your race plan, not the hours slider"). With the live **Adjust** feature (lengthen/shorten any session from the plan), an upfront hours budget is no longer worth the friction. The `weeklyTrainingHours` / `weekdayTrainingHours` fields remain optional on `OnboardingState` and every consumer no-ops when absent (`applyHoursBudget` gated `!= null && > 0`; budget banner returns `''`; training-horizon falls back to neutral). The "Your Week" preview now shows per-session lengths + a single Weekly total, plus an Adjust reveal line: "Run lengths follow your race plan. You can lengthen or shorten any session later from your plan."

2. **Workout preview step** (`workout-preview.ts`): Shown after schedule, before review. Displays the session types in the user's plan — easy, long, threshold, VO2 intervals, fartlek, and marathon pace (half/marathon only) — with one-line descriptions and science citations. Easy is always locked ("Required"). Long is locked for half/marathon targets (foundational); unlockable for 5K/10K. User can tap other cards to opt out. Exclusions stored in `onboarding.runningExcludedWorkouts` and applied at plan generation.

**Plan engine wiring**: `PlanContext` gains `excludedSlots` (filtered from quality priority before allocation) and `weeklyHoursTarget` (post-processes intents via `applyHoursBudget` — scales easy sessions first, then long run; never shortens quality sessions). All plan-display `generateWeekWorkouts` calls pass these from `s.onboarding`. **Two-tier floors** (added 2026-05-05): standard floors (easy 20m / long 40m) used first; soft floors (easy 15m / long 30m) used when standard would overflow; below the soft floor, residual overflow is accepted and surfaced via the plan-view budget banner.

**Plan-view budget banner** (`src/ui/plan-view.ts` `buildBudgetBanner`): when this week's rendered plan exceeds the user's stated weekly hours by > 5 min, a notice card surfaces above the workout list with two CTAs — Accept shorter sessions (records dismissal at current hours value) or Increase to N h/week (auto-bumps the target and clears the dismissal). Banner re-arms whenever `weeklyTrainingHours` changes.

**Flow**: `goals → connect-data → race-target → schedule → workout-preview → review` (running). Cycling unchanged.

**Key files**: `src/ui/wizard/steps/schedule.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/steps/workout-preview.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/controller.ts`, `src/workouts/plan_engine.ts` (`applyHoursBudget`), `src/workouts/intent_to_workout.ts` (populates `estimatedDurationMin`), `src/ui/plan-view.ts` (`buildBudgetBanner`), `src/workouts/generator.ts`, `src/types/onboarding.ts`
**Tests**: ✅ All 1375 passing — 7 new soft-floor tests in `src/workouts/plan_engine.budget.test.ts`

### 7d-ii. Race calendars roll forward (event pickers)

**What it does**: The marathon, half-marathon and triathlon pickers never run dry. Each curated race carries one confirmed date. Once that date passes, the race reappears as its next edition on the same weekday-of-month slot (Berlin = last Sunday of September, Chicago = 2nd Sunday of October, Boston = 3rd Monday of April). Projected dates show "(est.)" in the dropdown and, once picked, a caption: "Estimated from last year's date. Confirm on the race website before relying on it." Refreshing a date in the data file replaces the estimate with the confirmed date. A picked race's date is frozen onto state at pick time, so refreshing the list never moves an existing plan. **HYROX is not rolled forward** (venues and months move each season, decided 2026-09-28): it lists confirmed dates only and needs a manual refresh from hyrox.com each season. **Refreshed 2026-10-12 (lane H1, `29d45b3`)**: 50 confirmed 2027 events, January to the World Championships in Hong Kong on 2027-06-10 (the list ended on 2026-12-18); 10 late-2026 start dates corrected and 10 newly dated late-2026 events added; Paris 2027 (Grand Palais), Las Vegas 2027 (Las Vegas Convention Center) and Guadalajara 2027 (Expo Guadalajara, 1566 m) resolve to their own venues through event-level overrides (`getVenueIdForEvent`, `src/data/hyrox-events.ts:233`). Tests ✅ `src/data/hyrox-events.test.ts` (10). Cycling events use month hints, so they are unaffected.

**Key files**: `src/data/race-calendar.ts` (`nextEditionDate`, `withNextEdition`, `ESTIMATED_DATE_NOTE`), `src/data/marathons.ts`, `src/data/triathlons.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/steps/race-target.ts`, `src/ui/wizard/steps/triathlon-setup.ts`

**Tests**: ✅ `src/data/race-calendar.test.ts` (9: weekday-slot projection for last-Sunday / nth-weekday races, weekday preserved for every curated race, no curated race left in the past, ids preserved)

### 7d-iii. Running race page: near races, plan note, low-base marathon switch (2026-10-07)

**What it does**: The marathon and half race page no longer refuses a race closer than a full build. It lists every curated race from tomorrow to 52 weeks out and accepts a custom date from tomorrow (picker bounds tomorrow to the last date whose plan stays within 52 training weeks; "Pick a date from tomorrow to continue." otherwise). One muted line under the date says what the plan will be, picked from the calendar layout the plan is built on (`racePlanNote`): "16 weeks of training" for a full build; "6-week plan, short of the 8-week marathon build. 4 weeks of race-specific training from current fitness, then a 2-week taper."; "3-week plan, short of the 4-week half marathon build. 2 weeks of race-specific training, then race week."; and taper-only, race-week-only and "Too close for a primer session" variants. A marathon block that cannot build marathon endurance in time (a beginner, logged runs but none in the last 30 days, or projected long runs under 1:55; `lowBaseMarathon`, which reads the same `marathonEnduranceGap` check as the forecast) adds a plain line, e.g. "6 weeks cannot build marathon endurance. Long runs reach about 1:06, short of the 2:45 marathon-readiness long run, so the forecast finish time is optimistic." (since 2026-10-08 a duration under an hour reads in minutes, "about 36 minutes" rather than "0:36", via `fmtDurationMin`, `utils/format.ts:147`, shared with the forecast endurance-gap statement), and two buttons: "Half marathon on the same date" and the first listed marathon far enough away for a full build ("<race> · <date>"). One tap switches the selection and recomputes the plan length. Tristan's decision of 2026-10-06: allow these runners, say it plainly, offer the switch. The check never fires once the projected long run reaches 2:45. Race dates parse as local dates (`parseRaceDate`, `marathons.ts`), which fixes a race showing a day early west of UTC. Continue stays enabled for any date from tomorrow.

**Known**: the race page judges the block's projected peak long run, not the raw 30-day longest, and cannot see an hours target (the schedule step comes later); the switch is offered on the race page only; the note does not describe a partial first week (ISSUE-362, ISSUE-363). The plan that follows is described in 8.2.

**Key files**: `src/ui/wizard/steps/race-target.ts` (`renderBlockNote`, `customDateBounds`), `src/ui/wizard/steps/race-target-plan-note.ts` (`racePlanNote`, `lowBaseMarathon`, `trainingWeeksForRace`), `src/calculations/race-day-durability.ts` (`marathonEnduranceGap`), `src/data/marathons.ts` (`getMarathonsByDistance` min/max weeks)
**Tests**: ✅ `src/ui/wizard/steps/race-target-plan-note.test.ts` (every note variant, low-base cases including the lowbase-racepage scenario's "about N minutes", local dates in London summer and winter, New York, Los Angeles, Sydney and UTC, race list includes near races and never today), `src/utils/format.test.ts` (`fmtDurationMin`), `src/ui/wizard/steps/race-target-custom-date.test.ts` (near dates continue with the note, today and past refused, picker bounds, low-base warning and half switch in jsdom). ❌ Not device-verified (iOS date picker untested).

### 7e. Onboarding Review — "Here's what we found" (confirm & edit)

**What it does**: The last wizard step before "Build my plan". Shows recent training volume, measured benchmarks (VO2max, LT pace; FTP/CSS in tri/cycling), athlete profile, and personal bests — every row editable inline so the user corrects anything before the plan generates.

- **Personal bests**: all four running distance rows (5K / 10K / HM / Marathon) always render in running mode, even when nothing synced — empty rows read "-- · Tap to add". Tap any row to type a time. The continue gate requires at least one PB (running-plan init fails otherwise), so the rows must always be present to be satisfiable. **Typed PBs count without the Save link (2026-10-05)**: Return commits an inline edit, a readable draft lights Build, and Build saves it first. An unreadable draft such as "22.30" shows "Use mm:ss, e.g. 22:30, to continue." (h:mm:ss for half and marathon) while typing and on leaving the field. An untouched editor is not re-saved, so a PB keeps its stored date.
- **Typed-input Continue gates, all modes (2026-10-05)**: every wizard step that takes a typed date, time or PB now says why Continue is blocked, next to the button; on HYROX setup, triathlon past race and manual entry a tap on the blocked button also scrolls to the missing field. Race target: a custom date under the distance floor (marathon 8 weeks, half 4 weeks) or past 52 weeks shows its reason under the field, the picker's min/max match the check, and a refused date is cleared from state. HYROX setup and triathlon past race: see H0 and T1. Manual entry (running): Continue takes its first tap while a PB field is focused (the experience notice used to push the button about 125 px down mid-tap), and unreadable PB text is flagged. Triathlon, cycling and HYROX custom date fields react to typing, not just the picker. Tests ✅ `src/ui/wizard/steps/`: `race-target-custom-date` (8), `manual-entry-continue` (5), `review-pb-draft` (5), `setup-date-input-events` (2). ⚠️ Not device-verified (race-date path checked in the iPhone simulator only).
- **Weekly volume row counts as typed km (2026-10-09, `fb4d226`)**: a saved edit of the recent-volume row (Save, Return, or Build with the edit open) writes `detectedWeeklyKm` and sets `weeklyVolumeConfirmed`, as manual entry and the no-data slider already did (`applyEditingDraft`, `review.ts:2832`). Before, the edit set only the km, so it seeded neither the plan's starting volume (`initialization.ts:799`) nor the volume ramp and weekly load target (feature 8, SCIENCE_LOG §DF). ✅ `src/ui/wizard/steps/review-pb-draft.test.ts` (a saved volume edit sets the flag). ❌ Not device-verified.
- **VO2max** (editable, added 2026-05-29): tap to enter 20–85 ml/kg/min → `s.vo2Override`. Flows through to LT and paces via `recomputeLT`.
- **Lactate threshold pace** (editable, added 2026-05-29): tap to enter mm:ss → `s.ltOverride` → `recomputeLT`.
- Manual VO2max/LT values auto-yield to a clearly-better derived estimate on the next launch refresh (CLAUDE.md "Manually-set Benchmarks Yield to Improvements"). A "Revert to measured" link on the "Set manually" caption clears the override immediately (added 2026-06-03).
- **Fitness placement** (added 2026-06-03, SCIENCE_LOG §BK + §BL + §BM): each measured number carries a "where you sit" line plus a position scale (`placementScaleBar`, modeled on the Resting HRV tile's `baselineRangeBar`) derived from real reference data via `src/calculations/fitness-placement.ts`. VO2max row → FRIEND registry age/sex percentile with the band's p5/median/p95 as scale anchors ("Higher than 72% of men aged 30-39"; hidden when age or sex unknown). "Field position" row (running mode, any target distance) → current fitness projected to the user's own target distance and placed in the runner population ("Faster than 87% of male runners", 35M race results, sex-specific tables with overall fallback) with the age grade + WMA classification below the bar ("69% of world-record speed for your age, regional class"; bands 90/80/70/60 = world/national/regional/local class, dropping out below 60%). The scale's fast anchor is the WMA open standard (`openStandardSec`), which equals the road WR at these distances. PB rows → same population percentile + age-grade/band footer per filled PB. FTP row (tri + cycling) → Coggan W/kg band with a 2.0-5.6 W/kg scale, only with a real bodyweight. HYROX review → previous finish vs the real S8 to S9 field of the previous race's format, by sex for singles and by the pair's division for doubles ("Faster than 66% of Open Singles women" for 1:30:00; lane H3b), with time scale. LT deliberately carries no placement (restates the VO2 signal). FRIEND/WMA placements hide rather than guess when age or sex is missing; the population percentile falls back to the overall table. **No-signal gate (added 2026-06-15)**: both the Field position row and the VO2max FRIEND placement bar are suppressed when `getPhysiologicalVdot(s).source` is `tanda-fallback` or `none` — i.e. when the VDOT is the bare `s.v` baseline (default 50, or a stale carried value) rather than anything measured. A no-data runner no longer sees "Faster than 81%" / a projected race time / a population percentile; the VO2max value still shows with its "rough estimate, add a PB or sync HR to refine" caption. The rows reappear the moment a real source lands (a PB flips the chain to `pb-median`; device VO2 / HR calibration / observed LT all rank above the fallback).

**Key file**: `src/ui/wizard/steps/review.ts` (`renderTriPbsSection`, `renderVdotRow`, `renderLtRow`, `renderFieldPositionRow`, `placementScaleBar`, `commitEditing`, `refreshContinueGate`); placement helpers in `src/calculations/fitness-placement.ts`, FRIEND norms in `src/data/vo2max-norms.ts`, WMA standards in `src/data/wma-age-grading.ts`, population tables in `src/data/runner-population-distributions.ts`.
**Tests**: `src/calculations/fitness-placement.test.ts` — ✅ Passing. ⚠️ Screen not yet device-verified.

### 8. Plan Generator
**What it does**: Builds the full weekly workout schedule — which runs, at what pace, for how long — based on your training phase (base/build/peak/taper), runs per week, race distance, and runner type. Also accepts `weeklyHoursTarget` (scales session durations to fit the user's stated time budget) and `excludedSlots` (removes user-opted-out session types from generation). **Race-week primer** (2026-06-17, §CE): the final taper week seats a short sharpener (`TAPER_PRIMER_THRESHOLD` 3×2 min for marathon/half/10K; `TAPER_PRIMER_VO2` 4×1 min for 5K) instead of a full quality block — the taper holds intensity but collapses volume. Non-final taper weeks now draw the lightest variants (`pickPhaseVariant` treats taper like base). Tri/cycling mirror this by gating the taper quality flavour to sub-threshold (`gateLowIntensity` in `tri-intensity-profile.ts`); HYROX already downgraded taper kinds.

**Whole-plan shape (2026-10-06/07, SCIENCE_LOG §CT)**: across a running race plan, a recovery (deload) week is never longer and never has more quality sessions than the week before it; the long run grows at most 10% over the longest long run of the previous 4 weeks (`LONG_RUN_MAX_GROWTH_VS_RECENT_LONGEST`, Frandsen et al. 2025, approved by Tristan 2026-10-06); the week after a recovery week resumes one step above the pre-recovery long run; taper long runs are cut from the long run actually prescribed; the long run is always the week's longest run (easy runs are never sized past it, and the hours budget trims it only by the overflow). Race week (the plan's final training week, whatever its label) has no long run: the race is the week's long effort, the primer stays, and the week has one prescribed run fewer than other weeks. Continuous plans are exempt from the race-week rule. The engine reads what earlier weeks prescribed by replaying them from the static plan inputs (`nominalHistory`). Effect: 1,092 swept plans show 0 rising deloads, 0 long-run jumps over 10% and 0 race-week long runs (were 554, 654 and 1,092 without an hours target). Low-baseline and hours-capped runners now peak lower (e.g. marathon 12 weeks, detrained 15 km, novice: 165 to 81 min), and the race-day forecast says so (feature 3). Known: on tight budgets a quality session can still be longer than the long run (ISSUE-354). Tests ✅ `src/workouts/plan_engine.plan-shape.test.ts` (1,386-plan property sweep plus units), `src/workouts/long-run-placement.test.ts` (race week exempt). ❌ Not device-verified.

**Typed weekly km sets the volume ramp (2026-10-09, lane k `5a58cf2`, SCIENCE_LOG §DF)**: the archetype-gated ramp from current volume (§AK: a weekly budget that starts at the archetype start and grows by a fixed percentage, and only ever lowers the race-target week) needs an athlete archetype, which only a Strava or Apple Health sync wrote. A weekly km typed at onboarding or saved on the review step now gets one: `typedVolumeArchetype` (`athlete-archetype.ts:340`) runs the sync classifier on the typed km, and `planRampArchetype` (`:363`, the synced classification first) is the one archetype for the plan engine (`planEngineStateInputs`, `plan_engine.ts:1041`), the end-of-plan forecast, the marathon Monte Carlo and the race page's low-base note. Inputs a typed number cannot supply never fire a classifier branch: under 10 km is sedentary-novice (10 km floor, 5% a week), 10 km or more is consistent-runner (1.05 × the typed km, 7% a week), and a marathon PB never changes either, so more typed km never buys a slower ramp or a lower start. Running plans only (`typedVolumeShapesPlan`, since `4991e58`). Measured (16-week marathon, 10K 45:00, 4 runs): typed 15 km prices weeks 2, 3, 5 and 6 at 118 to 145 TSS (was the untyped 256 to 278), typed 30 km at 209 to 257; typed 50 km builds the untyped plan (the race-target week sits under the ramp). A 6-week marathon block (4 runs, no logged runs) builds long runs of 90, 96, 102 and 109 min for typed 45, 50 and 60 km alike. ✅ `src/calculations/athlete-archetype.test.ts` (`typedVolumeArchetype`, 6 new), `src/state/initialization.load-target-no-history.test.ts`, `src/state/typed-km-mode-gates.test.ts`. ❌ Not device-verified.

**Race-week placement and taper makeups (2026-10-07)**: every running race plan runs on race day's calendar and follows the race-day rules (feature 8.3): nothing on or after race day, the primer 3 to 5 days out, no long run or other quality session in the last 6 days, no back-to-back hard days in the last 13. A skipped long run or quality session is never carried into a taper week (skip push, week rollover and the generator all drop it, with the base skip penalty; `isTaperCarryBlocked`, `filterTaperMakeups`); an easy makeup enters a taper week only at its taper easy length and under the week's taper ceiling (`taperMakeupRoom`, `fitTaperEasyMakeups`). Tests ✅ `src/ui/skip-taper-carry.test.ts`, `src/workouts/race-day-rules.test.ts`.

**Key files**: `src/workouts/generator.ts`, `src/workouts/plan_engine.ts` (`TAPER_PRIMER_*`, `pickPhaseVariant`, `planWeekSessions`, `nominalHistory`, `isRaceWeek`, `applyHoursBudget`), `src/workouts/tri-intensity-profile.ts` (`gateLowIntensity`), `src/workouts/race-day-rules.ts`
**Tests**: `src/workouts/generator.test.ts`, `src/workouts/plan_engine.taper-primer.test.ts`, `src/workouts/plan_engine.plan-shape.test.ts` — ✅ Passing

### 8.1 Plan Phasing
**What it does**: Assigns Base / Build / Peak / Taper labels to every week of a plan with a single rule that scales coherently from 4-week sharpening blocks up to 50-week double-periodization arcs. Shared across running, triathlon, and HYROX via a `PhaseConfig`-driven engine; each format supplies its own caps and ratios.

- **Very short (4–7w)**: sharpening block (no real base — fitness cannot be built in a month). Since 2026-10-07 a running marathon under 8 training weeks or half under 4 takes the dedicated sharpening-block layout (`computeRunningPlanPhases`, feature 8.2), and in every format a plan too short for one week of each phase allocates the taper first: 1 week = [taper], 2 weeks = [peak, taper] (were [build] and [build, peak]). Running marathon plans of 2 or more weeks taper at least 2 weeks (`MARATHON_MIN_TAPER_WEEKS`), so the 8-week marathon is 2/3/1/2.
- **Single arc (8–32w running; 8–27w tri/hyrox)**: capped phases (running: taper ≤ 3w, peak ≤ 4w, build ≤ 8w; tri: taper ≤ 2w (the race taper is fixed by race distance since 2026-10-14, feature T2), peak ≤ 5w; hyrox: taper ≤ 2w, peak ≤ 3w). Base absorbs the remainder.
- **Double periodization (≥33w running; ≥28w tri/hyrox)**: cycle 1 (~45%) ends in a **Checkpoint** time-trial week, 2-week inter-cycle taper, cycle 2 (~55%) full Base → Build → Peak → Taper arc into the race. Phase Timeline shows the honest two-arc story.

When the current week is the checkpoint, the Phase Timeline shows a teal caption telling the user to race a parkrun or 10K time trial that Saturday. The result feeds VDOT/CSS/FTP auto-refresh through the existing activity matcher.

Format-specific configs preserve canonical splits at default plan lengths: running marathon 16w → 6/6/2/2; tri 70.3 / 20w → 8/6/4/2; tri Ironman / 24w → 10/7/5/2; HYROX / 18w → 7/6/3/2.

**Triathlon intensity distribution is race-distance-aware (2026-06-15)**: beyond volume, the *kind* of work adapts to the race format via `src/workouts/tri-intensity-profile.ts`. `TRI_QUALITY_SLOTS` sets how many weekly slots per discipline are quality (sprint/olympic intensity-led; Ironman a single quality slot in build/peak, the rest aerobic) and `TRI_QUALITY_FLAVOUR` sets which hard kind (short course = VO2/speed/anaerobic incl. a new `'vo2'` run; Ironman = sub-threshold sweet-spot/tempo/threshold only). Bricks scale by distance (`TRI_BRICK_POLICY`: sprint peak-only → Ironman build+peak with the largest run share). Seiler 80/20-by-time is preserved at every distance. SCIENCE_LOG §BV. ✅ tested (`tri-intensity-by-distance.test.ts`), ❌ not device-verified.

Replaces the legacy running-mode `>16w racePhaseStart` block-cycle prefix and extends double-periodization to tri/hyrox (neither had it before).

Science basis: Bompa & Buzzichelli 2018, Pfitzinger & Douglas 2009, Daniels 2014 (single-arc caps); Issurin 2010, Tønnessen et al. 2014 (double periodization); Mujika & Padilla 2003 (inter-cycle taper). Full derivation in `docs/SCIENCE_LOG.md §Z`.

**Key files**: `src/workouts/phases.ts` (`computePlanPhases`, `PhaseConfig`), `src/constants/triathlon-constants.ts` (`computeTriPlanPhases`), `src/constants/hyrox-constants.ts` (`computeHyroxPlanPhases`), `src/workouts/generator.ts` (`initializeWeeks`), `src/workouts/plan_engine.triathlon.ts`, `src/workouts/plan_engine.hyrox.ts`, `src/state/persistence.ts` (migration), `src/ui/stats-view.ts` (`buildPhaseTimeline` + checkpoint caption)
**Tests**: `src/workouts/phases.test.ts` (23, incl. taper-first in every format, block layouts marathon 1 to 7 and half 1 to 3, the 8-week marathon 2-week taper, full plans unchanged at every other length) + `src/constants/tri-hyrox-phases.test.ts` (11) — ✅ Passing
